# amigaplayer-rs

A Linux port of **DeliTracker 2.32** (Delirium Softdesign, AmigaOS, freeware since
2000), written in Rust with SDL3.

DeliTracker itself is closed source, so this is not a recompile. It reimplements
the main program, and runs the original 68k **DeliPlayers** (the plugins that
know the ~150 module formats) on an embedded 68020 emulator, the way UADE does
for EaglePlayers. It runs the **Eagleplayers** of Eagleplayer 2.06 too:

```
  module file ─▶ engine ──▶ native ProTracker ──────────────┐
                   │                                         ▼
                   └─▶ 68k DeliPlayer (m68k crate) ──▶ Paula emulation ──▶ mixer ─▶ SDL3 audio
                          │      ▲                    NoteStruct voices ─┘
                          ▼      │
                   HLE AmigaOS: exec, dos, utility, cia.resource, audio.device
                   DeliTrackerGlobals + dtg_* support functions
```

* `src/deli.rs`: the DeliPlayer interface (`DTP_*` tags, `DeliTrackerGlobals`,
  the `dtg_*` support functions), following the original developer kit.
* `src/eagle.rs`: the Eagleplayer additions: `EP_*` tags, the
  `EaglePlayerGlobals` (a separate block from DeliTracker's, since the two
  differ from `dtg_NotePlayer` on), the `ENPP_*` jump table below it, and the
  amplifier interface (`EP_InitAmplifier`, `ENPP_PokeAdr/Len/Per/Vol`,
  `ENPP_DMAMask`), whose writes go straight to Paula as in UADE's score.
* `src/engine.rs`: module identification and the player call sequence
  (Config → Check → ExtLoad → InitPlayer → Volume/Balance → InitSound →
  Interrupt/StartInt → …), subsongs, song end, mixing and filters.
* `src/machine.rs`: CPU, guest heap, A-line trap stubs, host→68k calls.
* `src/os.rs`: high-level emulation of the AmigaOS calls players make. Files
  map to the host filesystem, and unknown libraries become logged stubs.
* `src/paula.rs`, `src/paulaout.rs`, `src/cia.rs`, `src/bus.rs`: Paula's
  audio DMA state machine and its output stage (resampling and the A500/A1200
  and LED filters), the CIA timers, and the custom-chip address space, all on
  one colour-clock timebase (see Timing and sound).
* `src/notes.rs`: the renderer for `_note` players, which hand DeliTracker a
  NoteStruct instead of programming Paula, and the translation of that
  NoteStruct into the form a NotePlayer genie asks for. The structure is
  undocumented. Its layout was recovered by disassembling `SoundFX13_note` and
  the 4Voc, 8Voc and Headphone NotePlayer genies.
* `src/protracker.rs`: native ProTracker/NoiseTracker/SoundTracker replayer
  (it is built into DeliTracker rather than shipped as a plugin), a translation
  of ProTracker 2.3D's replay routine after 8bitbubsy's pt2-clone and PT2.3F
  (BSD 3-Clause: `LICENSE-pt2-clone`; see Built-in ProTracker).
* `src/decrunch.rs`: PowerPacker (PP20) decrunching, and in `src/decrunch/` the single-file depackers of libxmp (Pack-Ice, XPK SQSH, StoneCracker S404, MMCMP) and the ports of LibAncient, NostalgicPlayer's C# port of Teemu Suutari's ancient (CrunchMania, PowerPlayer, and the XPK container with the BZP2, BLZW, LHLB, MASH, NUKE, DUKE, RAKE, SHRI and SMPL methods; BSD-2-Clause and MIT: `LICENSE-ancient`).
* `src/prowizard/`: packed ProTracker modules converted back to ProTracker
  (see ProWizard).
* `src/ui.rs`: SDL3 window with transport controls, scopes and module list.
* `crates/m68k-rc`, `crates/players-native`, `tools/recomp`,
  `tools/native-players.py`: the optional 68k → Rust translation of the players
  (see Setup).

The engine is also a library (`src/lib.rs`). With `default-features = false` it
builds without SDL3, which is how other programs embed it:

```toml
amigaplayer-rs = { path = "../deliplayerrust", default-features = false }
```

```rust
let mut e = amigaplayer_rs::engine::Engine::new(48000.0);
e.load_players(&amigaplayer_rs::default_players_dir().unwrap());
e.play_data(path, bytes)?;      // or play_file(path)
e.render(&mut buf);             // interleaved stereo f32
```

## Setup

```sh
cargo build --release
tools/fetch-deliplayers.sh        # downloads DeliTracker232.lha from Aminet,
                                  # installs ~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/DeliPlayers
tools/fetch-eagleplayers.sh       # optional: downloads Eagleplayer.lha (2.06),
                                  # installs ~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/Eagleplayers
```

`fetch-deliplayers.sh` also installs the DeliGenies of the archive in
`~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/DeliGenies`.

The player binaries are not part of this repository. They are copyrighted
freeware and are fetched from the official Aminet archives
(`mus/play/DeliTracker232.lha`, `mus/play/Eagleplayer.lha`). Needs `curl` and
`lhasa`, plus the SDL3 development libraries for building.

### Native translation (optional)

The players can also be machine-translated from 68k to Rust, so their calls run
as native code instead of on the interpreter:

```sh
tools/recomp/build.sh                      # builds m68k-recomp into target-recomp/
python3 -I tools/native-players.py \
    ~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/DeliPlayers ~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/Eagleplayers
cargo build --release
```

`tools/recomp` is a static 68000/68020 → Rust recompiler (from the
m68k-recomp project). `tools/native-players.py` runs it over every hunk file it
finds, each at its own fixed address in `$800000-$AE0000`, and writes
`crates/players-native/gen` (derived from the player binaries, so it is
.gitignored). Players that need a 68020 are translated in 68020 mode, and those
the recompiler cannot represent are left to the interpreter. `--only NAME,...`
and `--max-cpu 68000` narrow it down.

At load time a player whose file hash matches a translated one is placed at its
translated address and run through the generated code. The interpreter
(`crates/m68k-rc`) covers whatever the recompiler did not reach, and A-line traps
go to the HLE as before. The translation is used only while the translated
instruction bytes are unchanged, and a player that modifies its own code falls
back to the interpreter. Without `gen/` the build is the plain interpreter.

`DELI_NATIVE=0` keeps the native memory layout but interprets everything, which
is how the translation is checked: renders with and without it must be
byte-identical. `--wav` prints the share of instructions run natively.
`DELI_WRITE_LOG=FILE` logs every custom-chip and CIA write of a `--wav` render
(`ADDRESS VALUE COLOUR-CLOCK`, the format of the comparison log of the UADE
build below). `DELI_DMA_LOG=FILE` logs every audio DMA word fetched (`CLOCK CHANNEL ADDRESS WORD`).
`DELI_MEM_DUMP=FILE` writes the guest memory as it is when a `--wav`
render ends, to diff two runs. `DELI_UADE=1` departs from the Amiga where UADE does, to compare
renders with it (see Timing and sound).

## Use

```sh
amigaplayer-rs [FILES/DIRS...]                     # player window
amigaplayer-rs --wav out.wav --seconds 60 MODULE   # render to WAV, headless
amigaplayer-rs --players DIR ...                   # other DeliPlayers dir
amigaplayer-rs --eagleplayers DIR ...              # other Eagleplayers dir
amigaplayer-rs --player NAME ...                   # only try this player
amigaplayer-rs --genies DIR ...                    # other DeliGenies dir
amigaplayer-rs --noteplayer 8Voices-NotePlayer ... # another genie plays _note players
amigaplayer-rs --noteplayer builtin ...            # the built-in renderer plays them
amigaplayer-rs --genie NAME ...                    # run any other genie
amigaplayer-rs --libs DIR ...                      # LIBS: for the players' disk libraries
amigaplayer-rs --wav out.wav ARCHIVE.lha           # every module in an archive, in order
amigaplayer-rs --archive-entry NAME ARCHIVE.lha    # only that entry of the archive
```

### Archives

A file given or dropped may be an archive: LhA / LZH (`-lh0-`, `-lh1-`, `-lh4-` to `-lh7-`,
`-lhx-`, `-lz4-`, `-lz5-`, `-lzs-`, `-pm0-`; header levels 0 to 3), Amiga LZX (stored and
crunched, merged-file groups, every file's CRC-32 checked), zip (stored and deflate),
tar, or a tar in gzip or xz. Its entries play in the archive's order, as a playlist; the
entries no player takes (companion files, texts) are skipped, and `.info` icons and texts are
not tried. In the window the archive's line is replaced by its playable entries when it is first
played; with `--wav` they are rendered one after the other, each up to `--seconds` or its song
end. `--archive-entry NAME` (amigaplayer-rs and amigaplayer-render) plays only the entry of that
name: its path in the archive, or its file name, in any case. A gzip or xz file of a single
module (`tune.mod.gz`) is not an archive: it plays as the module, beside its own companion files
(the decrunchers undo gzip and xz like PowerPacker). The LZX reader is this project's own code,
written from the format as `unlzx.c` documents it (Unlicense, in the repository
<https://github.com/nhoudelot/unlzx>; the file's own header names no licence: see
`LICENSE-unlzx` and the origin note in `src/archive/lzx.rs`). An entry whose CRC does not
match is skipped and listed, not played.

Modules in archives often need their companions from the same archive (`smp.*`, `*.ins`,
`Instruments/`, TFMX `mdat.*` with `smpl.*`). So an archive is extracted, whole, into a
directory of its own, and its entries are played from there: the players, the 68k ones
included, find the companions beside the module as they would on disk, with nothing changed in
how files are looked up. The directory is
`$AMIGAPLAYER_ARCHIVE_CACHE/<hash>-<size>` (default `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/amigaplayer-rs/archives`,
else `~/.cache/amigaplayer-rs/archives`), named after a hash of the archive's bytes, so a second
visit reuses it; the 200 most recently used are kept, older ones are deleted. Entry names are
made relative (no `..`, no volume, no absolute path) before anything is written, archives
larger than 512 MiB are refused, entries larger than 128 MiB and archives past 256 MiB
extracted are cut, and nothing in the directory is ever run. A virtual directory in the
emulated OS (the library's in-memory companions) was the other choice; it would not reach
libxmp's loaders and the 68k players' own file look-ups in the same way, and the files
on disk let a user look at what was played.

Players are looked up in `--players DIR`, `$DELIPLAYERS`,
`~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/DeliPlayers`, then `./DeliPlayers`; Eagleplayers in
`--eagleplayers DIR`, `$EAGLEPLAYERS`, `~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/Eagleplayers`, then
`./Eagleplayers`. DeliPlayers are asked first, Eagleplayers last (after the
SoundTracker heuristic), so installing them only adds formats.
The folders of the older name, `~/.config/deliplayer/...`, are still found after
the `amigaplayer-rs` ones.
`--players /nonexistent` leaves the DeliPlayers out.

Genies are looked up in `--genies DIR`, `$DELIGENIES`,
`~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/DeliGenies`, then `./DeliGenies`, and are named by their
internal name (an unknown name lists them). `_note` players are played by
4Voices-NotePlayer, DeliTracker's default, when it is installed, and otherwise
by the built-in renderer (`--noteplayer builtin` chooses that one). A genie
plays them on Paula the way it would on an Amiga. 4Voices plays four voices, so
by default players with more go to the built-in renderer; `--noteplayer
8Voices-NotePlayer` or `32Voices-NotePlayer` mix them on the 68000 instead,
which takes seconds to start and a lot of emulated CPU time. The 4Voices, 8Voices, 14Bit,
32Voices and Headphone NotePlayers work. A genie that fails is dropped, and the
built-in renderer takes over.

### Disk libraries

A library a player opens that the host does not emulate is loaded from disk when a
file of that name is found, and run on the emulated 68000 as AmigaOS would: LoadSeg,
the RomTag in its segments, InitResident (with `RTF_AUTOINIT`: MakeLibrary from its
vector table, InitStruct table and init routine; otherwise its own init routine),
then its Open vector on each OpenLibrary (CloseLibrary calls its Close). The name
is looked for as given (any volume such as `LIBS:` dropped, so
`replayers/x.library` keeps its folder), then as its file name alone, names in any
case, in:

1. the module's directory,
2. `libs/` in the module's directory,
3. `libs/` in the directory above it (an archive's `modules/` beside its `libs/`),
4. LIBS: `--libs DIR`, else `$AMIGAPLAYER_LIBS`, `~/.config/amigaplayer-rs/Libs`,
   `~/.config/deliplayer/Libs`, `./Libs` (amigaplayer-render: `--libs DIR` only).

The libraries the host emulates (exec, dos, graphics, utility, intuition, the CIA
resources, audio and timer devices) and the system libraries players already work
with as stubs (reqtools, asl, gadtools, icon, diskfont, the math libraries,
identify and the like) are never loaded, nor are decrunchers (refused, so players
fall back to plain data). When no file is found the library is the stub it always
was: its calls answer 0 (stonepacker.library's LVO -30 a handle). A library that
opens another goes through the same path. The libraries are the user's own files,
loaded at run time; none ship with this program.

You can drop files or folders onto the window, or use **Add** / **Dir**.

| Key | Action |
| --- | --- |
| Space | play / pause |
| Enter, double-click | play the selected module |
| Up / Down, PgUp / PgDn, mouse wheel | move through the list |
| Left / Right | previous / next subsong |
| P / N | previous / next module |
| S | stop |
| + / - | volume |
| R | random order |
| F | A500 / A1200 filter model |
| M | mono |
| O | open file dialog |
| Del | remove the selected entry |
| Q / Esc | quit |

**Next@End** moves on when a player signals song end. The **LED** button
honours the Amiga's LED filter, which players switch. **Sep-/Sep+** adjust the
stereo separation. **Mono** mixes both channels to mono.

`RUST_LOG=info` shows which player took a module. It also logs, once each, any
AmigaOS call that isn't implemented, which is where to look when a player
misbehaves.

## Status

Verified by rendering and comparing spectrograms against xmp:

* ProTracker modules (native replayer)
* FastTracker 2 XM and ScreamTracker 3 S3M (the original 68k players)
* AHX (the original 68k `_note` player)

All 85 players in the archive load, configure, and run their check routines
against every test file without crashing the host.

Not yet verified, for lack of test modules: most of the other formats (Future
Composer, SoundMon, TFMX, JamCracker, MED, …). Also unverified are players that
install their own CIA or audio interrupts instead of using DeliTracker's timer.

Eagleplayer 2.06: 112 of its 118 player files load (the four `AYPlayers` are
Z80 emulators for PlayAY, and BFB Master and Datatype have no module check).
A player without a check would take every module, so it is loaded only when chosen
with `--player` (or with `DELI_CHECKLESS=1`).

BFB Master (`--player BFB`, "BFBPlayMaster.Library 1.0" front end) plays through
`bfbplaymaster.library` and its `replayers/bfbplay*.library`, loaded from disk (see
Disk libraries; the BFB archive's `modules/` beside its `libs/` and
`libs/replayers/` finds them). With the archive's 1996 libraries: Oktalyzer
(bfbplayOKTA), ProTracker M.K. (bfbplayPT20), DigiBooster (bfbplayDIGI) and the
THX module in the AHX 1 format (`THX\0`, bfbplayTHXS) play, their 20 s renders' level
envelopes correlating at 0.69 (8 voices on the host amplifier against 8 mixed
by the library), 0.997, 0.94 and 0.999 with the built-in replayers'. The
`THX\1` (AHX 2) modules stop with a divide by zero in bfbplayTHXS's interrupt
code a few ticks in: it takes entry 51 of a 64-long pointer table of its own that
it never wrote (in its first setup nor in the second), and divides by a word read
through that null pointer. Observed black-box (memory watched, no OS call or
answer involved), and the one `THX\0` module plays, so the likely reason is that
the library predates the AHX 2 format; not a host gap. Game Music Creator
files and David Whittaker modules are refused by the master library itself
("InitPlayer of BFB failed"): it compares the module's first long and the word
at 12 with its format signatures (`GMOD`, ...) and these have none.
Rendered over the test modules with no DeliPlayers loaded, 46 play audible
music, and where a DeliPlayer covers the same format most renders correlate at
0.9 or better with the DeliPlayer's. Those that ask for the Eagleplayer
amplifier with more than 4 voices or without `EPAMB_Direct` (Fasttracker II,
PS3M with its S3M, MTM and 5-32 channel MODs, OctaMED, Oktalyzer, Digibooster,
8-voice Art of Noise) play all their voices through the host's amplifier
(`src/amplifier.rs`), a clean-room mixer "compatible with the 8 Bit
Amplifier": it keeps up to 32 virtual voices (Paula's semantics when poked,
the AudioStructs otherwise), mixes them in integers into two 8-bit buffers in
chip memory, and plays those on Paula channels 0 (left) and 1 (right) by DMA,
double-buffered, the next one mixed on channel 0's audio interrupt. Its
settings are the 8 Bit Amplifier's factory defaults: mix rate 28000 Hz less
500 for every voice past 2 (24978 Hz for 8), LRRL sides where the player sets
none, boost 1.0, 1.1 from 5 voices, 1.2 from 9 and so on; so these play quieter than a
4-voice module, and with 8-bit output. With `DELI_WRITE_LOG` the voices'
pokes are logged before mixing, at `0xD00000 | voice << 4 | register`
(register 0/2 LOC, 4 LEN, 6 PER, 8 VOL, `0xA` DMA, `0x8000` on), and
`tools/replay-cmp.py` compares them as `VLOC0`.. `VDMA31`; the amplifier's own
Paula writes are not logged. Players with 4 Direct voices or fewer still poke
Paula itself, as before. DigiBoosterPRO needs AHI and stays silent, and PlayAY,
which runs as a process of its own, does not work (its ZXAYEMUL and ZXAYAMAD tunes are played
by the built-in Z80 and AY emulation instead, see Open replayers). MusiclineEditor (a process
player too) plays: its InitPlayer branches on the flags `dtg_AudioAlloc`
returns, which the host sets as a `moveq #0,d0` would.

TME (V2.3, Promax's beta) claims many files of other formats and plays them
silent: its `EP_Check5` looks for 256 zero bytes after the module's tables, or,
in a file too short for that, at the words past the file's end, and never
compares what it computes with `dtg_ChkSize`, as the later TME player UADE
ships does. That is the player's own check, not the host's (forced on those
files in UADE it plays them silent too). So, as UADE's eagleplayer.conf does,
the host asks TME only about files named as TME modules (`tme.*` or `*.tme`);
the others (in the coverage sample about 1,900 of the archive's tunes:
OctaMED SoundStudio, Sierra AGI, Sonix, early TFMX, Sonic Arranger) are left
to the other players, or refused. Some formats have an Eagleplayer 2.06 that does not
know the variant the archive holds: OctaMED and MED refuse MMD3 modules in
mix mode (`flags2` bit 7, all of OctaMED SoundStudio's `oss.*`; the open replayers
`OctaMED SoundStudio` and `OctaMED` play them), MusicMaker8
the single-file `FORM MMV8` modules, and Mark Cooksey, SoundMaster, Sound
Images, Jesper Olsen and Sean Conran the replayer variants of the files they
refuse (their checks match code or data patterns of other variants; UADE plays
them with later versions of these players).

Players that run as a process of their own (`DTP_Process`: IFF-8SVX, AIFF,
RIFF-WAV) work. The process is a guest task with its own stack that the host
suspends whenever it `Wait`s for a signal it has not received. DeliTracker's
calls reach it as DeliMessages on the port `DTP_MsgPort` names, and it resumes
when an `audio.device` write it waits for has played. `audio.device` queues
writes per channel and replies to each after `ioa_Cycles` passes through its
sample, following Paula's audio interrupts. It honours `ADIOF_PERVOL`,
`ADIOF_WRITEMESSAGE`, `ADIOF_SYNCCYCLE`, `CMD_STOP`/`CMD_START`,
`ADCMD_WAITCYCLE`, and allocation precedence: a higher precedence steals
channels, and the loser's request fails with `ADIOERR_CHANNELSTOLEN`. Processes
run in priority order. These sample players stream from the module file, which
they open through `dtg_PathArrayPtr`, so that holds the module's path when
`InitPlayer` runs.

NotePlayer genies are processes too. DeliTracker does not give them the
player's NoteStruct but one of its own, built from the genie's
`DTP_NotePlayer` record (voices per side, NSTF flags, maximum frequency). It
puts each channel on a side, converts periods to frequencies in Hz and word
lengths to byte lengths, and refills that NoteStruct before every
`DTP_NoteSignal`.

## Timing and sound

The aim is the sound of a real PAL Amiga 500. Everything runs on one clock in
colour clocks (3,546,895 Hz):

* Guest code takes the time a 68000 at 7.09 MHz takes: every instruction adds
  its cycles (68000 User's Manual tables, `crates/m68k-rc/src/cycles.rs`) and a
  custom-chip access happens at the time it is made. The generated code
  (`m68k-recomp --cycles`) and both interpreters count with the same table, so
  native and interpreted renders stay byte-identical. Chip RAM contention is
  not modelled. The time a player takes to get ready (AHX precalculates for
  about 10 s on a 68000) passes before the song starts, not as silence in it.
* Paula is UADE's audio state machine: DMA starts at the next line, one DMA
  word per channel per line, the length reload and its interrupt, attached
  channels, and the interrupt a channel without DMA raises. Fed the register
  writes and DMA fetches of a UADE run, it produces UADE's samples to the bit.
  It departs from UADE where UADE departs from the hardware: volume 64 is full
  volume (UADE plays 63), and periods below 16 are played as written (UADE
  raises them to 16).
* The output stage is UADE's default one: each channel's output averaged over
  the colour clocks of an output sample, hard left/right, then the A500 filter
  (fixed 6.2 kHz RC plus 20 kHz) or the A1200 one (none), and the LED filter
  (CIA-A PRA bit 1) on top. It matches UADE's `--write-audio` dumps to the bit.
  Formats from the PC (XM, S3M, 669, WAV, AIFF, MP3, TakeTracker) skip the
  filters, since their music was not made for them. **F** switches between the
  A500 and A1200 models.
* Frames are 313 lines (49.92 Hz vertical blank; UADE uses 312), the CIA
  timers count E-clock ticks (colour clock / 5), DeliTracker's timer
  interrupt comes every timer value + 1 E-clock ticks, and `dtg_WaitAudioDMA`
  (and `ENPP_DMAMask` switching DMA on) waits for 10 new raster lines, as
  UADE's score does (DeliTracker's own wait is not known). The built-in
  ProTracker waits as ProTracker 2.3D does, a DBRA loop of 300 (1515 colour
  clocks, 6.7 lines on a 7 MHz 68000), before switching the new notes' DMA on,
  and again before setting the repeats.
* `_note` players go through a NotePlayer genie (4Voices by default) and so
  through Paula like everything else; the built-in renderer, used without one,
  keeps its own filters.

Compared with UADE (`DELI_UADE=1`: 312 lines, UADE's volume and period
quirks, hard panning) on the same player binaries, the tick timing matches to
the colour clock and the register writes of a tick land within a few dozen
colour clocks of UADE's. Renders still differ by a few samples of phase per
channel, because UADE's CPU is a 68020 at `cpu_speed=max` with UAE's cycle
tables, not an A500's 68000.

`src/paula.rs` and `src/paulaout.rs` follow UADE's `audio.c` and are derived
from GPL-2 code.

## Output stages

Everything audible passes through some of these stages. With `--wav` the
program prints the ones in effect for the file on stderr, after every
automatic choice (`output: backend=... filter=a500(auto) led=auto(player)
sep=0.70 ...`), and a second line telling whether the player switched the LED
filter during the render (`led-switched: yes at 1.23s (off, 2 change(s); led
on at start)`).

| Stage | Models | Default | Used by | Switch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paula resampler (`src/paulaout.rs`) | UADE's "default" resampler: each channel averaged over the colour clocks of an output sample (48 kHz), hard left/right (channels 0, 3 left; 1, 2 right) | on, always | every Paula backend: 68k players, Eagleplayers, built-in ProTracker, open replayers | none |
| Filter model | A500: fixed RC low-pass 6.2 kHz plus 20 kHz; A1200: none; none | `auto`: A500, and none for PC formats (`PC_FORMATS` in `src/engine.rs`: 669, ADPCM, AIFF, WAV/RIFF, MPEG/MP3, FastTracker, ScreamTracker, PS3M, TakeTracker) | Paula backends | `--filter a500\|a1200\|none\|auto`; **F** in the window toggles A500/A1200 |
| LED filter | the Amiga's LED low-pass (7 kHz biquad), on while CIA-A PRA bit 1 is low; an Amiga starts with it on, UADE with it off | `auto`: follows the player (`--led on` is the same) | Paula backends, except where the filter model is none | `--led auto\|on\|off\|force`: `off` ignores it, `force` keeps it on whatever the player writes |
| Separation | mixes a fraction of each side into the other: 1 = hard Amiga panning, 0 = mono (`Settings::separation`); UADE-compat (`DELI_UADE=1`) sets 1 | 0.7 | Paula backends (libxmp's own mix is 100 %) | `--separation V` |
| Mono | sums both sides | off | all backends | `--mono` |
| Volume | output gain; UADE's level is 1 | 0.8 (= libxmp's full scale on its backend) | all | `--volume V` |
| 8-bit amplifier (`src/amplifier.rs`) | Eagleplayer's "8 Bit Amplifier": voices mixed sample-and-hold at the mix rate (28 kHz less 500 Hz per voice past 2) onto Paula channels 0 and 1, boost 1.0 + (voices-1)/4 tenths (10 + (n-1)/4 in tenths, scaled by the player's gain), clipped to 8 bits | on, for the players that ask for it | the Eagleplayers that call `EP_InitAmplifier` (the software-mixing ones) and the open replayers that port them | none (printed as `amplifier=8bit@...Hz,boost=...`) |
| Built-in NotePlayer renderer | its own one-pole 4.42 kHz low-pass and LED biquad (3.275 kHz) in software, outside Paula | used only with `--noteplayer builtin` or when no genie fits | `_note` players | follows `--filter`/`--led` (a500 and LED switches); printed as `builtin-voices=...` |
| libxmp mixer (`src/xmp`) | libxmp 4.7.4's 16-bit stereo software mixer: volume ramps, anticlick, separation 100 | linear interpolation | the PC-tracker formats of the libxmp port; no Paula, no Amiga filters, no LED | `--interp nearest\|linear\|spline` |
| libxmp resonant filter | the IT/LIQ/IMF lowpass (`QUIRK_FILTER`) | on (only modules that use it are affected) | libxmp backend | `--xmp-filter on\|off` |

Other places: the wasm crate (`crates/amigaplayer-wasm`) renders through
`open::Song`, so it has the engine's default settings (A500 filter, LED
following the player, separation 0.7, 0.8 volume); libxmp formats there are the
same mixer. There is no DC removal or dithering anywhere, and the only clipping
is the final `clamp(-1, 1)` of the f32 mix (and the 8-bit amplifier's).

### Comparing with the originals

`tools/refcmp.py` renders every file of the listen page's list with the open
replayer and with its original player under the same output settings, and
compares the PCM:

```sh
python3 -I tools/refcmp.py --all -j 8            # whole list; non-zero exit on a NEW inconsistency
python3 -I tools/refcmp.py --fmt tfmx --id 101   # a few formats / ids
python3 -I tools/refcmp.py --all --update-baseline
```

The original is the same binary playing the real Eagleplayer/DeliPlayer on the
emulated Amiga (so the output stage is identical and bit-identical output is
possible); for the libxmp-port formats it is libxmp itself. Each file gets a
class (IDENTICAL, LAG-ONLY, NEAR, SIMILAR, or an inconsistency: FAIL,
STAGE-MISMATCH, SILENT, LENGTH, FILTER, LEVEL, PITCH, TEMPO, DIFF, with the
thresholds as named constants at the top of the script). `tools/refcmp-baseline.tsv`
holds each file's class; a run reports new and fixed inconsistencies against it.
Where the register writes of a port fall at other times than the original's,
expect SIMILAR rather than IDENTICAL.

## Built-in ProTracker

`src/protracker.rs` translates ProTracker 2.3D's replay routine, after
8bitbubsy's pt2-clone (its C port of the 2.3D replayer) and his PT2.3F
re-source of 2.3D (BSD 3-Clause, `LICENSE-pt2-clone`). The engine calls it
from DeliTracker's timer interrupt, and it writes Paula's registers through the
bus as the 68k routine does, in its order:

* a row: per voice the period when the previous note long was empty, DMA off,
  length, pointer and period of a new note, the period or volume its effect
  sets, its volume; then 2.3D's DMA wait (a DBRA loop of 300, 1515 colour
  clocks), DMA on for the new notes, the wait again, and the repeat pointer and
  length of all four voices, the last voice first. The first row comes on the
  sixth interrupt, as in ProTracker;
* between rows, each voice's effect writes and volume;
* the writes spaced as the 68000 takes from one to the next (estimated from
  the cycle counts of the 2.3 code paths, not measured);
* periods from ProTracker's tables (16 finetunes, and what an arpeggio on
  finetune -1 reads past them), the LED filter off at the start (as the 68k
  hosting and UADE leave it; an Amiga powers on with it on; E0x switches it), a new tempo from the next
  tick (the CIA latch), and 2.3D's quirks: 9xx added twice, B after D on one
  row clearing the break row, E6x and EEx on delayed rows, the tremolo ramp
  testing the vibrato position, the 12-bit wrap of portamentos, EFx inverting
  the sample in memory, F00 ending the song. E8x does nothing (pt2-clone's
  default, PT2.3F). An empty sample's note takes the replay's null-pointer path
  (length 1, no repeat), as in the 2.3D editor, where it has no memory.

Checked against UADE's PTK-Prowiz (a ProTracker 3.0b replay): in uade123 for
plain modules (its check wants the module type UADE's uade.library passes;
A500 filter, LED forced on unless the module uses E0x, as UADE starts it off),
in this engine for the ProWizard families (see ProWizard); 20 s each, 300
random archive MODs, NostalgicPlayer's ProTracker and NoiseTracker test files
and 25 or 15 of each SoundTracker folder's, 638 played (59 the reference
refuses, 20 other players take):

| | State identical | The same writes in the same order, but the repeats' order | specdist median, to 2 kHz |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| before | 392 | 0 | 5.20 dB, 2.01 |
| now | 480 | 329 | 2.43 dB, 2.06 |

(7 more files, StarTrekker modules with no tick to compare, count as identical
in both.) Strict order never matches whole files: PTK-Prowiz sets the repeats
first voice first, 2.3D last voice first. It also plays SoundTracker modules in
a NoiseTracker-like mode (the period in every effects tick), so they match in
state only. Of the periods that still differ first, most are PTK-Prowiz
starting a portamento a tick later than 2.3D does; others are 0-length
samples, which PTK-Prowiz does not play. Its DMA waits are 10 raster lines,
2.3D's 1515 colour clocks.

8-channel MODs (`CD81`, `8CHN`, `FLT8`) are not ProTracker's: DeliTracker's
TakeTracker and PS3M players take them and mix in software, so the built-in
replayer leaves them alone.

## Open replayers

`src/replay/` holds Rust ports of NostalgicPlayer's players (MIT, Thomas
Neumann: `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`; the AHX one is a port of 8bitbubsy's
ahx2play, BSD 3-Clause: `LICENSE-ahx2play`; the HivelyTracker one follows Pete Gordon's
hvl_replay, BSD 3-Clause: `LICENSE-hivelytracker`; the PolyTracker one is a port of
libxmp, MIT, Claudio Matsuoka and Hipolito Carraro Jr: `LICENSE-libxmp`; the Soundtracker Pro II one reads files as OpenMPT's `Load_stp.cpp` does,
BSD 3-Clause: `LICENSE-openmpt`), open-source stand-ins for the 68k players.
DeliTracker's DeliPlayers and genies may not be modified or shipped in another
project; Eagleplayer's main program and engines are GPL since 2.04, but its
external Eagleplayers are not ("The external Eagleplayers are not released under
the GPL", Eagleplayer 2.04 sources), and most of them hold the composers' own
replay routines.
`--open-replayers` plays the formats they know with them instead of the 68k
players. They write Paula's registers as the original players do, and are
checked against the Eagleplayers, not against NostalgicPlayer:

```sh
DELI_WRITE_LOG=ep.log amigaplayer-rs --player 'Pumatracker 1.0' --wav ep.wav MODULE
DELI_WRITE_LOG=rs.log amigaplayer-rs --open-replayers --wav rs.wav MODULE
python3 -I tools/replay-cmp.py ep.log rs.log   # tick by tick; first difference
```

`tools/replay-batch.py` does this for a list of modules and sorts them into
identical, differing and refused.

The write log's first line says where the module was loaded, so sample
addresses compare as offsets in the module. Within a tick the ports write all
at once where 68k code takes about a millisecond, so renders differ in phase
while the register writes are the same.

| Format | Port of | Checked against | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| PumaTracker | NostalgicPlayer PumaTracker | Pumatracker 1.0 (Eagleplayer 2.06) | 60 of 61 modules identical for 20 s; `puma.toki7` plays a note past the period table, where the original reads its own variables (period `$5060`, silent) and the port writes 0 (silent) |
| SidMon 2.0 | NostalgicPlayer SidMon 2.0 | SIDMon 2.0 (Sidmon-II Eagleplayer V2.3, 22 Aug 97) | 109 of 109 modules identical for 20 s (the 110th file of the list is an XM); `Flipper - Title` plays note 80, past the period table, where the original reads its own pointers to the sample list, which the port keeps too; renders of 12 modules within a median 1.0 dB (specdist) of the reference |
| JamCracker | NostalgicPlayer JamCracker | JamCracker V1.33 (Eagleplayer; the DeliPlayer JamCracker_note has the same name, so run with `DELIPLAYERS` pointing elsewhere) | 63 of 63 modules identical for 20 s (12 more of the list are other formats) |
| FutureComposer1.3 | NostalgicPlayer Future Composer and its 1.3 converter | FutureComposer1.3 (Eagleplayer 2.02) | 148 of 152 modules identical for 20 s; 2 more are identical but for a sample address past the module's end, which `replay-cmp.py` takes for player memory; `pow2` (one tick, volume 0) and `Thr Demo Disk 17` read the player's sample table past its periods or its samples, whose contents depend on where the player was loaded |
| FutureComposer1.4 | NostalgicPlayer Future Composer | FutureComposer1.4 (Eagleplayer 2.02) | 113 of 114 modules identical for 20 s; `Tristar - Scoopex` plays sample 230 of 90 (at volume 0), which the original reads past its data hunk and the port as empty |
| SoundMon 2.0 | NostalgicPlayer SoundMon (its "1.1", mark `V.2`) | SoundMon (Eagleplayer V5.02) | 216 of 219 modules identical for 20 s; the two Green Berets are identical but for an empty sample at the module's end, which `replay-cmp.py` takes for player memory; `NR.1` plays an instrument past the 15, whose sample address the original reads past its table and the port takes as its empty sample (same lengths and volumes, here 0) |
| SoundMon 2.2 | NostalgicPlayer SoundMon (mark `V.3`) | SoundMon 2.2 (Eagleplayer V2.1) | 70 of 72 modules identical for 20 s; `Green Beret` as for 2.0; in `test3` a looped sample makes the original call into its own code (an ENPP call through the wrong register), which writes `$33` into the unused top byte of a sample address |
| Delta Music 2.0 | NostalgicPlayer Delta Music 2.0 | DeltaMusic 2.0 (Eagleplayer, V2.2) | 103 of 103 modules identical for 20 s and for 90 s; it reads the tables and the saved editor state from the module, as the original does, and spaces its writes as the original does (specdist mean 1.8 dB) |
| Fred Editor | NostalgicPlayer Fred Editor and its Fred Editor (Final) converter | Fred (Eagleplayer V2.3, 25 Aug 97), which plays only the final modules (the editor's replayer with the song), with a replayer of its own | 54 of 54 modules identical for 20 s and for 90 s, and on 9 later subsongs of 5 modules; it works on the replayer's variables and the voices' synth waveforms in the module, as the original does, and spaces its writes as the original does (specdist median 0.77 dB over the 54, 2.68 before); of the list, 2 Fuzzball modules have a replayer the Eagleplayer does not recognise, 4 are editor files (`Fred Editor `), which it does not play either |
| SoundFX1.3 | NostalgicPlayer SoundFX and its 1.x converter (mark `SONG`) | SoundFX 1.3 (Eagleplayer V2.1, 20 Dec 96) | 327 of 327 modules identical for 20 s; renders of 12 modules within a median 0.96 dB (specdist) of the reference (47.7 dB with no time between the writes); where a DMA off to on gap is shorter than a word at the voice's period, the restart can differ (the reference's ticks start later, by its init time), as in `AA Demo 1` (3.8 dB) |
| SoundFX2.0 | NostalgicPlayer SoundFX (mark `SO31`) | SoundFX 2.0 (Eagleplayer V2.1, 20 Dec 96) | 89 of 89 modules identical for 20 s; renders of 12 modules within a median 1.35 dB (specdist) of the reference (25.9 dB with no time between the writes). The original tests the flags `dtg_GetListData` returns, which the host now sets as UADE's does |
| MultiMedia Sound | Written from a behaviour specification (register-write logs of the reference); the SoundFX 2.0 file layout (mark `SO31`) with its own engine; claims a module only by file name (prefix `mms.` or `sfx20.`, or suffix `.mms` or `.sfx20`), any other name goes to SoundFX2.0 | MultiMedia Sound (mms.library 6.4 replay routine, Eagleplayer `MultiMedia_Sound`) | every register write (address, value, order, colour clock) equal to the reference in 32 reference logs (see the commit message) |
| Delta Music 1.0 | NostalgicPlayer Delta Music 1.0 | DeltaMusic 1.0 (Eagleplayer V2.1) | 9 modules (1 more file of the list is another format): 1 identical for 20 s; the other 8 differ only in the period, at volume 0, of a voice that has not played a note yet, whose arpeggio the original reads from its code hunk's pointer to its data hunk, so from where it was loaded. With the reference's pointer in that place all 9 are identical for 20 s and for 90 s. The variables live in guest memory in the original's layout, and the writes come within a few colour clocks of the original's; specdist mean, median of the 9: 0.30 dB (0.03 with the reference's pointer, 8.4 without the waits) |
| Sonic Arranger | NostalgicPlayer Sonic Arranger and its "final" converter | SonicArranger (Eagleplayer V4.11; the DeliPlayer SAplayer_note has the same name, so run with `DELIPLAYERS` pointing elsewhere) | 263 of 263 modules identical for 20 s ("SOARV1.0", offsets-only and with the replayer in front; the 43 others of the list are lh-packed `@OARV1.0`, Lionheart's, or other formats, which the original refuses too), subsongs too; writes spaced by the original's code-path costs fitted to its logs; renders of 10 modules within a median 0.94 dB (specdist; 1.44 dB with flat gaps). The song end comes on entering the last position, as the original signals it (21 modules ending within 20 s, `trsi1.sa`, `snake4.sa`, the Ambermoon songs among them, now end on the original's tick, not up to 195 ticks later); a one-position song alone ends when its position is over, as the later SonicArranger Eagleplayer (UADE's) signals it, since the original ends it after its first row (`sa.199`, Marvin's Marvellous Adventure, ended after 0.1 s, and plays its 7.8 s); the 263 stay identical for 20 s played on past either side's song end. The Lionheart offsets-only files with 2, 3 or 5 subsongs (`sa.level_6`, `sa.endgegner34.SA`) or an empty last part (`sa.sfx`) the original refuses (its check takes a subsong table only when its size's low byte has no bits but 2 and 3, so one subsong), and the port takes them as UADE's newer player and NostalgicPlayer's converter do (no such test): 29 modules of the 40 `sa.*`/`*.sa` files of the archive that the port refused before (the others are 8 lh-packed, 2 XMs and `sa.hole2`, not Sonic Arranger). Their master volume starts at 64 and a note starts with period 1, the interrupt cleared and DMA off, as UADE's log shows; of the 29, 12 write the same audio registers as UADE for 20 s (times and sample addresses aside) and 17 differ (a voice's DMACON is written with 0 for a voice that is off, and a row starts a tick later than the port's, in ways not worked out); the modules taken before stay identical |
| SidMon 1.0 | NostalgicPlayer SidMon 1.0 | SIDMon 1.0 (Eagleplayer V4.01, adapted by DEFECT; the DeliPlayer Sidmon1.0_note plays these first, so run with `DELIPLAYERS` pointing elsewhere) | 37 of 52 modules identical for 20 s; the other 15 read periods past the table (mostly instrument 0, 32 bytes before the instruments, with a stray finetune), where the original reads past its code hunk and the port writes no period; with those masked all 52 are identical. (Since the TFHD work the port starts voices 0-2 with the leftovers the reference writes on its first music tick; later ticks of channels that stay unused may still differ.) Channel 3's DMA bytes, the mixed buffers, are identical to the original's for 14 of 15; in `Turrican 2 - Title` the first SetPrevNote of a mixed voice reads note 0, not the leftover, and three buffers (489-491, about 8 s in) differ until its next note. | Hippel TFMX | NostalgicPlayer Hippel (its TFMX modules), the replayer at the module's start | Hippel (Eagleplayer 2.06, which calls the module's own replayer) | 13 builds of the replayer, told apart by a hash of their code up to its empty table (the bytes after it are the module's saved variables): the song data at $A94 (two code versions), $8D6, $8E6, $962, $98E, $9A6, $A2C (two), $A4C, $A50, $A58 and $B02. They share the song data and differ in which effects their code has (read as NostalgicPlayer reads them: a new note turning the voice's DMA off, the E5/E7/E9 variants, the vibrato's version) and in how they order their Paula writes: the $A94, $A2C, $A58 and $B02 ones write each voice's period and volume with its effects and wait for the line (1 to 3 times) before the DMA on and the loops, the $8D6 to $A50 ones write every voice's effects, the DMA on and the loops, then all periods and volumes. Of the 71 `sog.*` and `hip.*` modules of the Hippel game folders 70 play, 67 identical for 60 s (3000 ticks) and their next three subsongs for 20 s (52 runs): 22 at $A94, 2 at $8D6, 2 at $8E6, 4 of 6 at $962, 6 of 7 at $98E, 6 at $9A6, 15 at $A2C, 1 each at $A4C and $A50, 7 at $A58, 1 at $B02. The three others differ in 0.5 to 3.4% of their ticks (from tick 3, 1179 and 2050): the reference writes a restarted voice's loop one tick late there, which depends on Paula's state and which the port does not model. The $A3A build (`hip.boot_loader`), whose volumes the port does not reproduce (they go past 64), is refused and left to the Eagleplayer. 10 `hip.*` modules saved while a song played (the word six bytes before the song data is set) are played as UADE's player plays them (Wanted Team's Jochen Hippel V1.0, which plays them as any module), the Eagleplayer starting no song for them: the same writes without the Eagleplayer's first DMA stop in the early builds, and a subsong change that also sets the four volumes to 0; all 10 identical to that player (run in amigaplayer-rs) for 20 s (1000 ticks), and the next three subsongs of the 8 with more (20 runs). `uade123` itself writes the loops of restarted voices a tick later and turns the DMA on later, here (6 to 854 of 1000 ticks identical, with a 68000 at real speed too) as in modules the Eagleplayer plays (3 to 999 in 5 of them). Fields InitSound leaves alone come from the module's saved voice variables ($A94) or its pending loops (the early builds). Writes spaced by each build's code-path costs, fitted to the reference's logs; InitPlayer's time as measured (8297 colour clocks and 9 per sample and 58.4 per subsong), the DMA on after a model of its VHPOSR polling (DMA off to on within 30 colour clocks of the reference in 64 to 100% of the cases of each build, within 120 in 82 to 100%); renders of 12 modules within a median 0.03 to 2.0 dB (specdist) |
| Hippel 7V | NostalgicPlayer Hippel 7V (its TFMX and COSO modules), following the original's replayer | JochenHippel-7V (the Eagleplayer UADE ships, "Jochen Hippel 7V player module V1.3", Wanted Team, 68020), a replayer of its own: voices 0-2 on Paula channels 3, 1 and 2, voices 3-6 mixed in software into the two buffers channel 0 plays, its tick run from channel 0's audio interrupt; the mixing rate 24 kHz, as UADE's `EP-Hippel_7V.cfg` sets it (the player's own default is 16); amigaplayer-rs gives the Eagleplayer that file too (`Env:` and `EnvArc:` are the `ENV` folder of the Eagleplayer directory), and tools/refcmp.py takes UADE's player first, as EP206's is the older V1.0 | 16 modules (NostalgicPlayer's set and the UnExoticA Amberstar, Ghost Battle and Lethal Xcess folders): 11 TFMX ones (the song data at the file's start, or after a game's replayer) and 5 COSO ones. The 11 TFMX ones: register writes identical tick by tick for 20 s (992 to 1221 ticks; the last, cut by the run's end, not compared); channel 0's DMA words, the mixed buffers, identical for 2, and for the 9 others but for one buffer at the start (the third, about 30 ms in), where the reference plays the first mixed note's first block as silence and the port does not (the reverse in `hip7.game_end`), not tracked down. Later subsongs (5: Amberstar Ending and Intro 2, Australian Pioneers 2 and 3, Lethal Xcess Intro 2) the same after the subsong change, Australian Pioneers 3 up to UADE's song end (512 ticks). Subsong counts as UADE's; song ends within 0.1 s of UADE's in all 17 subsong runs of the 11 that end (201 to 501 s, and 10 to 70 s), and in the 9 of the COSO ones against their twins' (subsong 2 of Amberstar Ending ends in neither within 512 s). The Eagleplayer refuses the 5 COSO modules; the port plays them with the same replayer, their tracks read as NostalgicPlayer reads them: writes identical to the reference of their TFMX twins, the mixed buffers differing where a sample starts, as the COSO files hold a nonzero first word in samples the TFMX files start with 0. A note past the period table reads the mixer's variables (72-83) or the original's code (84 and up), where the port reads 0. Writes spaced by code-path costs fitted to the reference's logs (one constant per path, not per effect: 34% of the write times within 10 colour clocks, a median 62 off), the DMA on after a model of its VHPOSR polling; renders of the 11 within a median 0.37 to 4.0 dB (specdist, both with the A500 filter). Before, the clean TFMX ones were taken by the TFMX 1.5 detection and the COSO ones played as 4-voice Hippel COSO |

Writes spaced by code-path costs fitted to its logs, and the start by the Eagleplayer's 68000 cycles: the module-dependent part of its InitPlayer (a song-length scan of the module, loops over the instruments and their names) is counted per code path, so InitSound's first write falls on the reference's colour clock for all 483 modules and the Paula line phase the rest follows is the reference's. 91% of the writes of 47 modules are on the reference's clock and all within 2 colour clocks (the rest are 1 off, in runs of ticks from the second); renders of the first 10 50 Hz modules are 0.00 dB (specdist, median, every module; 2.65, 0.28 to 4.19, before), 47 modules 0.00 median, the worst 1.32 |
| PreTracker | Raspberry Casket (Chris Hodges) and rePlayer's C player (Daniel Collin), both MIT (`LICENSE-PreTracker`; released with permission from Pink / Abyss) | UADE's PreTracker player (Pink's V1.5 replay; it has no check, so it needs one that accepts the module) | 109 of 109 modules identical for 20 s, 10 of them for 180 s, and 6 subsongs of 2 V1.5 modules (numbered 0 to n-1 as the reference does); the port signals a song end where the reference plays on. The waves are generated at init as Raspberry Casket does, in the original's 8-bit integer arithmetic (the C player uses floating point): 139 of 139 modules (the 109 and 30 more) byte-identical in chip memory against the reference loaded alone; in `prt.m065` a wave at base note 117 has a phase step that passes its oscillator table, and the original reads its own memory up to 64K past it: the port holds that memory as far as the wave reads it (the player's computed tables built, 82 single bytes of UADE's player file embedded on the project owner's decision, `src/replay/pretracker_player.bin`; zeros behind the player, which is what the original loaded alone has there, while in a full Eagleplayer directory another player's hunk follows). Where the two sources and the reference differ, the port follows the reference: first row 15 ticks, ping-pong forwards, 32-bit chord phase, V1.0 subloop wait, word vibrato delay. Writes spaced by the original's work per tick fitted to its logs over 37 modules (rms 309 colour clocks); the wave generation takes no time, so the song starts mid-frame; renders of 10 modules within a median 2.5 dB (specdist; 0.56 to 4.13 by module; the format is very sensitive to the write timing) |
| Musicline Editor | NostalgicPlayer MusiclineEditor (MIT, Thomas Neumann; `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`), 4 and 8 voices; the tables are NostalgicPlayer's | MusiclineEditor (Eagleplayer; UADE's score for the mixing) | 4 voices: 162 of 192 modules identical for 20 s against UADE's write log; on the other 30 every tick both play is identical but the port plays fewer of them in 20 s (547 to 1880 against 951 to 2021), (its tick durations follow the Eagleplayer on a 68000, UADE's 68020 runs at maximum speed; the build before the first-tick and tick-duration commits matched 189 of 192); against the Eagleplayer's own write log (empty player and genie directories, so the heap layout is the same; the port's data hunk is at the top of memory like the original's and differs by one constant) 74 of 192 are identical and 53 more have all but 3 ticks equal (the last tick cut by the 20 s window), the other 65 differ because tunes that overrun a 68000 tick take longer per tick in the Eagleplayer than the port's cost model gives (5 to 20 percent), so their ticks drift apart; no sample address differs any more except where the two runs played a different number of ticks. 8 voices (mixed in software, two voices to a Paula channel, from the channel-0 audio interrupt): the mix buffers of 112 of 113 modules are byte-identical to UADE's; the last one differs where UADE itself plays a buffer twice (a late interrupt). The 4-voice timer starts before its latch is written, so it counts down from $FFFF and the first tick comes 65536 E-clocks after the start, regular from there as in the Eagleplayer; each tick takes the time the original's takes before its first write (priced by what the tick does: each effect pass and byte, each row line and voice step; measured on the Eagleplayer's runs. On 174 four-voice modules, 130 fitted and 44 held out, the overrunning tunes' ticks now follow the Eagleplayer's to 0.06 percent at worst on the held-out ones, against up to 7 percent before, which the identical-tick counts above predate). The 8-voice mode is too heavy for a 68000: the original Eagleplayer's audio interrupt overruns its buffer on a 68000-timed machine, so in amigaplayer-rs's engine it swaps buffers only every second interrupt (roughly 283 swaps in 10 s, against 627 when the CPU keeps up). The port plays as on a CPU that keeps up, as UADE (68020, max speed) does. Its PAL buffers are byte-identical to a PAL UADE run on 112 of 113 modules; UADE itself runs the player as NTSC, because its score leaves dtg_GfxBase unset. The port follows the original where NostalgicPlayer differs (acknowledged interrupts, restart waits, word-multiply volumes, no range checks, no song end) |
| HivelyTracker | NostalgicPlayer HivelyTracker (HVL; AHX files stay with the AHX port), following Pete Gordon's hvl_replay where they differ (the position after the last is 0, a song ends by the original's flags); played on the Eagleplayer amplifier, two virtual voices (left, right) a channel for the pan | no Amiga player plays HVL (the THX Eagleplayer refuses it), so the original C replayer, hvl2wav (replayer 1.9), built with 32-bit types, dumped tick by tick (volume, period, pan and a hash of the 640-byte waveform loop of every voice) | 52 of 52 distinct modules (4 to 16 channels, speed multipliers 1 to 3, none with subsongs) identical to the song's end, with the reference run on the port's waveform tables: the reference's own differ from AHX's (its noise has $7F where AHX has $80, its filters are floating point where AHX and NostalgicPlayer settle integer filters; its plain triangles, sawtooths and squares are the port's byte for byte, its filtered ones differ by 1 to 12 steps on average). Renders against hvl2wav (20 s, 100 Hz to 2 kHz, level matched, 52 modules): mean 5.96 dB (3.45 to 12.10 by module), spectral correlation 0.77; the amplifier's output is 14.6 dB quieter (its own boost; the song's mix gain is not applied) and Paula's filters roll off what is above 2 kHz. Ring modulation is approximated: the modulating waveform is multiplied into the loop at the ratio of the periods, from the loop's start |
| 15-instrument SoundTrackers (Ultimate SoundTracker 1.0 and 1.8, SoundTracker II, VI and IX, Master SoundTracker, SoundTracker 2.x) | NostalgicPlayer's ModTracker player (MIT, Thomas Neumann; `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`): its identification of the variant and its playback; the modules have no tag, and the 31-instrument formats stay with the built-in ProTracker | UADE's Ultimate Soundtracker player for the two Ultimate SoundTrackers, its Soundtracker IV player for the rest | Against the references' write logs for 20 s, 100 archive modules a variant: Ultimate SoundTracker 1.0 100 identical, 1.8 100, SoundTracker II 98, VI 95, IX 5, Master SoundTracker 58, SoundTracker 2.x 33. The rest differ only where the reference lacks an effect (E, the filter; D and B in 2.x, where the reference's D is a volume slide; 3 to 8 in the later ones), so on 500 copies with those effects removed (effects 0, 1, 2, C and F kept): 1 of 1, 3 of 3, 121 of 123, 203 of 204, 154 of 159, and SoundTracker IX 2 of 10 with every tick the same but the count (the reference ignores the tempo byte that NostalgicPlayer, and so the port, takes as a CIA timer). What stays: an arpeggio on a period that is not in the table (the original reads on past its table into its own code and data, the port writes 0; 3 modules); a first pattern of 32 or more (the original's offset is a signed word and reads before the module; 5 modules); and a module the reference breaks off early. The port follows the references where NostalgicPlayer differs (a loop length of 0 words is a loop in the D.O.C. players; any sample puts a voice on the restore list; F00 stops the song; Ultimate SoundTracker's bend starts from the period of its own row). Renders of 10 modules within a median 0.1 to 0.4 dB (specdist; worst frame 4.6 dB) |
| SoundTracker 2.6 and IceTracker (tags `MTN\0` and `IT10` at offset 1464; `.st26`, `.ice`, `st26.*`) | NostalgicPlayer's ModTracker player (MIT, Thomas Neumann; `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`): its identification (the tag) and its loader's checks (the number of tracks is the highest track referenced plus one; no sample misses more than 512 bytes). A 31-instrument module whose positions name four tracks each, one per voice. `src/replay/st26.rs` lays the positions out as the patterns of a ProTracker module (position `p` is pattern `p`) and the built-in ProTracker plays it, its samples where they lie in the loaded module. Fxy follows the reference, not NostalgicPlayer's alternating odd and even speeds: a parameter under `$20` is a speed, `F00` does nothing, and two speeds play as the larger one at the tempo that keeps the rows' mean length (125 · 2 · max / (x + y), rounded), set on the next row when that row's cell of the voice has no effect (measured with synthetic modules in UADE; with a busy next cell the reference does something else, which the port does not follow: no tempo). A song length of 0 or over 128 is refused | UADE's PTK-Prowiz (`STK_2.6 - IceTracker`), run with its write log | 24 modules (NostalgicPlayer's 21 SoundTracker 2.6 and 4 IceTracker test files, one of them the same as UnExoticA's `st26.newweirdbu3`, and AMP's `ST26.targettitle`; 2 more, `Duncan 1` and `Test`, crash the reference) for 20 s, tick by tick: no run is register-identical in strict order, as for ProTracker modules (PTK-Prowiz writes every voice's period in every tick and sets the repeats first voice first, see Built-in ProTracker). 19 of 24 are identical in every tick without the period writes that leave a period as it is and with the repeats in voice order, and so in Paula's state. The other 5 agree for their first 5 to 1,078 ticks; then 4 start a portamento a tick before the reference (as the built-in ProTracker does for ProTracker modules) and 1 (`Base Boost`) plays a note of an empty sample, which PTK-Prowiz skips. The layout played by UADE as an M.K. module is not the same: PTK-Prowiz plays it as ProTracker, not in its SoundTracker mode. None of the 7,546 present of the corpus lists' 10,181 listed modules, nor of NostalgicPlayer's 967 other SoundTracker, NoiseTracker and ProTracker test files, is detected differently. No regression: 227 files (3 of each `ports/*.txt` list, 15 of the ProTracker and ProWizard lists, 3 of each of NostalgicPlayer's 14 other tracker folders) give identical WAV hashes before and after |

Its identifier knows only `FLT8`; the other tags have no source of their own (same layout, more cells to a row), no code from elsewhere. Four-channel tags stay with the built-in ProTracker. Played on the Eagleplayer amplifier, one voice a channel (Amiga LRRL sides). Not played: StarTrekker AM instruments, E8x (Karplus-Strong). Fixed where the source is wrong: period slides wrap past their limit, a tremolo's volume outlives it, FLT8's vibrato has no depth | the PS3M DeliPlayer (software mixer; no per-tick state to compare), and openmpt123 as a second opinion | 420 of 420 corpus modules (402 FST.* and others, 2 to 32 channels; 21 `4CHN` stay with ProTracker) play under their own format name with the player directories empty; none silent but one that is silent in PS3M and openmpt too. 40 spread over the tags, 20 s: level within 3 percent of PS3M (median); against PS3M mean 10.9 dB (specdist, aligned by cross-correlation, usually within 4 ms), spectral correlation 0.77, as close as openmpt123 is to PS3M (10.8 dB, 0.68); against openmpt123 14.0 dB, 0.77. Not bit-exact: PS3M's mixing rate and interpolation differ from the amplifier's. FLT8, OCTA, CD61, FA06 have no module in the corpus (FLT8 only a synthetic test) |
| Quadra Composer (IFF EMOD) | NostalgicPlayer QuadraComposer (MIT, Thomas Neumann; `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`; the tables are its own) | IFF EMOD (Eagleplayer) | 27 of 27 modules identical for 20 s (the archive's `emod.*`), 23 of 27 for 90 s: the other 4 (`OmenousChug`, `Enigma` and two more) differ in a few later vibrato periods. Where the original differs from NostalgicPlayer it is followed: the row's DMA goes off and on once for every voice a tick starts, with the loop registers of all four voices after it (0 and length 0 for a sample without a loop); a retrig or note delay starts its voice the same way; a sample of one word or less is written with length 0; the tempo's CIA timer is 1789773 / tempo; effect 6 writes the period without vibrato. Renders differ widely (specdist median 17 dB over 12 modules) though the writes are identical: after a sample without a loop the original's loop registers (address 0, length 0) make Paula play 128 KB from address 0, the engine's own memory, which differs between a 68k run and a port run |

Everything that goes to the voices' LOC, LEN and VOL matches in the cases examined; what differs is which ticks the 68k player writes a voice's period on and how many times (it writes it once or twice, by the row's effect, and every tick under some effects), which this port approximates from the rows seen (and the first row's, the loops after each row, and the E1x and E2x pairs are the Eagleplayer's). Subsongs: none | Oktalyzer (Eagleplayer), run with the DeliPlayer directory empty (the DeliPlayer `Oktalyzer` plays nothing here) | 450 distinct modules of the archive (CMOD of 4 single channels: 237; with mixed channels: 213), all played under their own name with the DeliPlayer, Eagleplayer and genie directories empty but one (truncated: the Eagleplayer refuses it too). The mixed channels' AudioStructs as the mixer reads them at each buffer (trace of the amplifier), when the port is given the Eagleplayer's own start-up times (the time from the amplifier's start to its first writes, to the restart and to the first tick, which depend on the module): 196 of 213 modules equal in every one of their 970 buffers, 99.5 percent of 201 212 buffers, and 194 of 213 renders sample for sample identical to the Eagleplayer's (specdist 0.000 median, 6.1 worst). Without them (the port's default, a fit of those times to the number of pattern cells, rms 350 000 colour clocks) the phase between the ticks and the buffers is not the original's, so a note may reach the mixer a buffer early or late: renders against the Eagleplayer's, 213 modules, specdist median 3.7 dB (xcorr lag; 4 renders with a wrong lag give 10 to 36); 4 single channels, median 0.8 dB. The Eagleplayer's InitPlayer time, which sets that phase, is not reproduced (it goes through the patterns; its cost is not a function of the module that fitted). A mixed voice whose note an effect moves above the table plays a period read past the original's table (not reproduced: it stops). No regression: 143 modules of the other 48 corpus lists play unchanged |
| OctaMED (mix mode, 5-8 channels) | NostalgicPlayer OctaMED (MIT, Thomas Neumann; `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`; it imports no code of its `Ports` directory): its loader (MMD0 to MMD3, the old songs' conversion to mix mode, the 5-8 channel songs' tempo conversion), player tick (rows, hold and decay, the effect commands, synth and hybrid sounds) and frequency table (`src/replay/med/mix.rs`), on the host's amplifier (`Hw::voice_*`): one voice a track, Amiga sides LRRL, at most 32 voices. A note is the voice's DMA off, the sample's start and length, the period for its frequency, the volume, DMA on and the loop; the amplifier got a LEN in bytes and a LOC at any byte (`Poke::LenBytes`, `LocByte`: a software mixer's samples are longer than Paula's 65535 words and loop at odd samples), and its mix rate is set to 31 kHz, the highest the emulated Paula plays. What the amplifier has no counterpart of: 16-bit and stereo samples become 8-bit mono when the module is loaded (the high byte, the mean of the two channels), in the module's own memory where no other sample lies; a ping-pong loop is unrolled into a loop of twice the length; a synth or hybrid sound's waveform is the voice's next block, taking effect where the block ends as on Paula; the echo (the song's or its one effect group's, normal or crossed) is a copy of each track's pokes a delay later (to the nearest tick) on a voice of the same side (crossed: the other) at the level of one repeat, 2 to the minus depth (the echo of the echo is not played, nor are several effect groups, and a song with more than 16 tracks has none); not played: stereo separation, the track pans (a voice keeps its Amiga side), "sample backwards" (command 20), MIDI instruments, the filter sweeps, the volume adjust, and the original's own mix rate and level (the amplifier's: every voice of a side may sound at full volume). Timing and pitch as UADE's players have them on a PAL Amiga: a tick of 3579545 / Hz colour clocks and periods from 3579545 / frequency (0.9% low), the older songs' ticks another 0.45% longer (fitted); but a 5-8 channel song's tick (tempo 1-10, whatever the BPM table says) follows the Eagleplayer OctaMED's table, a CIA latch of 90 * (100 + 10 t) (t above 10 is 10), (latch + 1) * 5 colour clocks: equal tick intervals to the Eagleplayer on all 7 songs of the corpus it plays (they were 0.9 to 1.7% longer) | Octa-MED (the 68k player that UADE (`uade123`) takes for MMD0-MMD2 songs; the Eagleplayer OctaMED refuses these songs), mixing in software; its renders only, no register writes to compare | 335 distinct modules of the archive (MMD0-MMD2 with the 5-8 channel flag or mix mode, `MED.*`), all played under their own name with the DeliPlayer, Eagleplayer and genie directories empty. Renders of 20 s against UADE's (specdist at the xcorr lag, level matched since the amplifier's scale is its own; two renderings of the same notes through the Paula path and through this one give a mean of 6.3 dB, 4.7 on the bins that carry energy): 334 compared, mean 6.36 dB median (p25 5.78, p75 7.26, p90 8.94; 279 under 8 dB, 8 over 12), spectral correlation 0.78 median; the older songs' tempo drifted 0.02 s in 4 s from UADE's before the fitted 0.45% (4 songs checked after: at most 0.005 s in 4 s, 0.01 s for the one 8-channel song of tempo 8). Not compared note by note: the original's mixer is software. 8-channel songs of tempo 1 to 9 (NostalgicPlayer's conversion table) still drift 0.15%. No regression: 1081 of the 1194 distinct modules of ports/med.txt (the 4-channel MMD0-MMD2 and the MED 2.10 files) and 145 distinct modules (3 of each other corpus list) render WAVs, 10 s each, identical to the previous build's; the 113 that differ are the ones it refused before (25 MED, 88 OSS) |

| MaxTrax (`mxtx.*`; Joe Pearce and Talin's Amiga music driver: Contraption Zack, SimAnt, SimEarth, Might and Magic III, Dune II, Legend of Kyrandia, A-Train, Darkseed, the SSI Krynn and Pools games) | A direct port of the Wanted Team's MaxTrax driver source (the EaglePlayer adaptation of 2007, LGPL-2.1-or-later, Copyright 1991 Talin (David Joiner) and Joe Pearce; `LICENSE-MaxTrax`; `src/replay/maxtrax.rs`): the MIDI-like score sequencer run once per 50 Hz frame (notes, tempo and its ramps, pitch bend, the controllers, program changes, repeats), four voices picked by priority and age, the patches' attack, sustain and release envelopes, the 68000 arithmetic of its period and volume calculations (word divisions that keep their operand on overflow, the antilog table), and its bugs kept as coded. The audio.device it writes through (CMD_WRITE of an attack and a looping sustain, ADCMD_PERVOL every frame, CMD_FLUSH) is the host's, reproduced in the port with the same start-of-pass and end-of-pass rules; the waveforms are copied to chip memory in the original's allocation order. A module with no samples takes them from `SMPL.<name>`, `SMPL.set` or `MXTX.main` beside it. Recognition is the Eagleplayer's: `MXTX`, a positive tempo, 1 to 256 scores each inside the file, at most 64 samples (so `jaws.mx` and `fx.mx`, which have no scores, are refused) | MaxTrax (Eagleplayer 2.06; register writes via `DELI_WRITE_LOG`) | Partly verified. The Eagleplayer plays 60 of the archive's 81 modules (its file requester for a companion file is not implemented, so the 17 A-Train modules, the two Kyrandia scores without samples of their own, and `jaws.mx` and `fx.mx` with no scores have no reference: the companion path and those modules are unverified against the original). Of the 59 with a sound, over 15 s of each, 14 give exactly the Eagleplayer's register writes (e.g. Leaf, MX03, MX10, MK2, march, guild.mx, and the 4-, 5- and 10-event scores of Dune II, the INTRO1A files and Kyrandia, which the Eagleplayer loops where the 2007 source plays them once, so the port loops every score, for 12 s except INTRO1A.ENG's last 5 ticks), 21 are identical in 90% or more of their ticks, 35 in half or more, and 9 in under 15% (17669 of 32997 ticks); the PER and VOL values, the note timing, the envelopes and the waveform addresses (up to one constant offset) agree before the first difference, which falls at tick 75 for the median module. Rendered and compared by refcmp (`--list` with a private list of the 59, 20 s each): 1 LAG-ONLY, 41 SIMILAR, 3 TEMPO (the port 40 to 70 ms ahead over 20 s), 10 DIFF, and 4 LEVEL, which are the 0.1 s and 0.7 s renders of Dune II's and Kyrandia's short scores that end at the song end. What differs is when Paula takes a voice's restart: a voice stolen and rewritten within less than one word-time (about 960 colour clocks) restarts or not depending on the phase of the old sample's word, which follows the Eagleplayer's start-up time (different for each module) that the port does not reproduce, so the sustain's start (a PER/VOL pair and the LOC/LEN of the loop) comes a few ticks earlier or later, and the voice is silent for a tick when it comes. Waits within a tick are fitted to one module (Darkseed MX01) and are off by up to a few hundred colour clocks elsewhere. No module is in the class lists or the baseline (`tools/refcmp-baseline.tsv` is unchanged) |
| SynTracker (`st.*`, `synmod.*`; four-channel synthesis tracker, modules start with `SYNTRACKER-SONG:`) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code (`src/replay/syntracker.rs`). Four voices at 50 Hz, each playing a 32-byte looping waveform that is rebuilt every tick (wavetable scanning with turn positions, low-pass morphing) or a plain 8-bit sample; envelope, vibrato tables, arpeggio, glides, fine slides, speed and jump effects, and the 128-byte working-to-playing waveform copy at the end of every tick in a chip buffer allocated for it. Timing inside a tick follows the specification's fitted gap tables; the row processing's cost is a per-channel constant. Decisions where the specification leaves the choice: reads outside the module or past the tables give 0; the vibrato search that finds no period leaves it as it is; the song-end flag is raised at the position wrap and the player goes on | SynTracker (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log, on 23 synthetic modules (the archive holds no real SynTracker file) | 23 of 23 modules: every Paula register write (address, value, order) identical to the reference in every compared tick (about 9,200 ticks, `tools/replay-cmp.py`); times within a tick: pitch and volume writes within a few colour clocks in most ticks, the row ticks' start writes off by up to a few hundred (the original's row cost depends on instrument and effect and is fitted as a constant); DMA off-to-on gaps differ by 300 to 460 colour clocks in about 3% of the restarts. Five modules and their stream hashes are unit tests. None of the corpus modules is claimed (the archive has none) |
| Sierra AGI (`agi.*`; the PC adventure games' music and sound effects, King's Quest, Space Quest and others) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code (`src/replay/agi.rs`). Four note lists (three square-wave voices of a 4-byte wave, a noise voice of a generated 4096-byte table), a 60 Hz tick (the 50 Hz reload times 5/6, 59115 colour clocks); a note is the sample pointer and length, period, volume and DMA enable; the song ends when every voice has ended, and a tune with an empty noise voice then starts again. The host does not tell the replayer the file's name, so recognition is by content alone: the header offsets and the end marker before each of the last three streams, as the player checks, and in addition every stream is walked note by note and must reach its own end marker before the next one starts (the player's check alone is a few bytes; the walk is the departure from the specification, and all 629 known files pass it). Not played: the specification's second replay variant (an envelope, a smoother wave), which the reference never uses | Sierra-AGI (the 68k player UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | 629 of 629 distinct `agi.*` modules of the archive give the same ordered Paula writes as UADE for 20 s (the audio registers and DMACON from the first tick on, sample addresses compared as the tone or the noise table, times not compared; at the end of a 20 s cut the two runs differ by up to 21 writes, less than a tick); 40 of 40 looping modules over three passes with UADE's song end ignored. None of the 7 546 present of the corpus lists' 10 181 listed modules is claimed. No regression: 3 modules of each `ports/*.txt` list give identical WAV hashes before and after |
| Sonix Music Driver (`snx.*`, `tiny.*`; Stellar 7, A-10 Tank Killer, the Carmen Sandiego games, Antheads, It Came From the Desert) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code (`src/replay/sonix.rs`). Four tracks of 16-bit events; `snx` songs tick at their tempo, `tiny` songs step every n-th interrupt of a tempo-table timer. Instruments are loaded from the song's `Instruments` folder in any case: synthesis (a 128-byte wave through 64 precomputed filters, mixed or widened by a second phase, envelope, LFO, glide), sampled sound (`.instr` and `.ss`), AIFF zones and IFF 8SVX. A sampled note mutes its channel, writes the first block, enables DMA after a nine-line wait and writes the loop block a tick later; a synthesis voice writes pointer, length, period and volume every tick into one of two 128-byte buffers. Recognition is by content (the format's two header tests; a file that starts `FORM` is left to the IFF-SMUS replayer); the file name is not checked, which the host does not give to a detector. The IFF-SMUS replayer is untouched and shares no code with this one (it keeps the original's guest-memory layout). Not played in any real song, so only built from the specification: 8SVX instruments, a voice changing technology mid-song, SNX synthesis | Sonix Music Driver (the 68k player UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | 109 of 109 modules (27 `snx`, 82 `tiny`) give the same ordered Paula writes as UADE for 20 s (the audio registers and DMACON from the first tick on, sample addresses compared relative to each instrument's data or the player's buffers, times not compared; up to 19 writes of a cut-off tick differ at the end), and 109 of 109 over 20 s with UADE's song end ignored (3 to 11 restarts). The words Paula fetched equal the sample data the port holds (13,764,548 words, sampled and AIFF) and 12,432 synthesised-wave passes equal the port's waves, but for the last two passes of `tiny.ida`'s log. None of the 7,546 present of the corpus lists' 10,181 listed modules is claimed; all 179 IFF-SMUS ones still go to IFF-SMUS. No regression: the 179 IFF-SMUS modules and 3 of each other `ports/*.txt` list give identical WAV hashes before and after (323 of 340 with audio) |

Recognition is `PTMF` at 0x2C after the DOS mark at 28 and counts in range. On the host's amplifier (`Hw::voice_*`), one voice a channel, sides from the channels' pans (0-7 left, 9-15 right, centre to the side with fewer voices; `8xx` and `E8x` cannot move a voice), the amplifier's boost raised as side^0.85 to match the Eagleplayer's level. 16-bit samples keep their high byte, bidirectional loops are unrolled, and the mix is the amplifier's sample-and-hold (no interpolation) | PolyTracker (Eagleplayer 2.06; it mixes in software, so no register log to compare) and libxmp itself (`xmp`, nearest, one channel at a time) | Not bit-exact. Against the Eagleplayer, 12 modules of 4 to 30 channels, 20 s, `specdist.py` at the xcorr lag: mean 4.7 to 11.3 dB, spectral correlation 0.80 to 0.92 (the Eagleplayer against OpenMPT on one module is 9.4 dB and 0.92), level within -2.2 and +1.1 dB. Against libxmp, 508 channels of the 111 modules soloed for 6 s (shifted 50 ms for the amplifier's delay): median spectral cosine 0.94, loudness-envelope correlation 0.93; the differences are the high-pitched samples (a 44 kHz hi-hat is aliased by the 27 kHz mix) and the 8-bit mix of quiet voices. The 111 PTMF files of the archive (81 `PTM.*`, 30 in `nostalgicplayer/`) all play as PolyTracker with sound with the DeliPlayer, Eagleplayer and genie directories empty; none of the 10 057 readable modules of the corpus lists is claimed |

| Dave Lowe (`dl.*`; Power Drift, The Games, Altered Beast, Frontier, Midwinter II and others, 1988-92) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code (`src/replay/davelowe.rs`, with `davelowe_tables.rs`: the per-game flags and sample-table fix-ups written out from the specification's tables). One four-voice engine for both formats: order lists of sequences, a word above 100 a note (period, length), a word of 100 or less a command (sample, envelope, portamento, vibrato, rest, next sequence, jump ...), a 20 ms tick, an envelope of volumes ended by 0x00FF whose last value is held, the song ends when every voice has wrapped its order list once. These files are hunk executables that carry the game's own replay routine; it is not run: the tables are read where the specification says, the per-game differences of the routine (about 30 builds) are a table keyed by the hunk size and three header offsets, and a few special cases (the Midwinter II repair streams, Time Scanner's start-up effect on channel D, the After Burner rest, Frontier II's fade-in, the sample-table fix-up of four modules). Recognition is by content: the exact hunk header and `UNCLEART` (or `DAVELOWE` for the two older flavours, which have no replay code in the file). Departures from the specification, from the black-box runs: Street Fighter II writes a volume of 1 before each held value; the Raffles and Soldier of Light routines, like those that return at a rest, do not write the held value; IK+ does (the table says the reverse); the older flavours' master volume does not count the rest's direct write of 0; command 52's boost lasts until the next fetch. F-15 Strike Eagle II subsong 2 reads a sample record from the middle of another, so what plays depends on the load address of the original run: the port takes its type word as the high half of the absolute address, never 0 | Dave Lowe (the 68k player UADE (`uade123`) ships; the Wanted Team Eagleplayer), run with its write log | 47 of 47 modules recognised as `Dave Lowe`. In the 82 runs that end before 20 s the song-end tick is the same as UADE's. None of the 7 546 present of the corpus lists' 10 181 listed modules is claimed. No regression: 3 modules of each `ports/*.txt` list give identical WAV hashes before and after |

They differ in the step clock, the note and pitch rules, and the samples (the second game embeds 8SVX samples). Engine C (Guldkorn and Kalas Puffs Expressen) is an Amiga executable image whose code is not run: its tables (song table, pattern base, instrument table, silent buffer, period table) are located structurally by generic instruction signatures, and its two code versions (the title music and the in-game music) are written from their behaviour: tempo steps, pattern items with prefix commands, per-note sample, note, volume and release lists, portamento. Recognition is by content (the data layout, or the branch table, init routine signature and period-table marker of the code images) and does not need the companion. Every sixth tick is a row tick; a note switches the voice off on the row tick and starts the sample on the next, and the loop registers are rewritten every tick. Commands: volume, stop, hold, fades, loops, repeat row, restart, ADKCON bits; filter and sync flags have no Paula effect and are ignored. The companion is `smp.NAME` for `sjs.NAME` (any case), else `smp.set`. Recognition is by content (the first row: no notes, one command on every voice). Departures from the specification: none known; notes above 39, instruments 0 or above 38 and reading past the song's end give silence (not exercised).
