Around it, what the player was seen to do (traced writes into the module, a memory dump of the voice block at the driver's first instruction, traced branches into the module): the pointers between the data block's offset 0x24 and the sequence list's end marker are moved from the link origin (the smallest one below the list minus the offset after the marker) to the load address, a four-record voice block gets the first entry's streams, 0xFF at offset 0x14 and its transposition bytes, the tick/speed/row/position variables get fixed start values (two B start states, told apart by the list's stride), and offset 0 is called each CIA tick with A0 = the module. The list, its stride, the wrap, the voice variable and the record size come from the driver's code shapes. The song end is the list's wrap (family A); the first call's time is fitted to the reference's start-ups. The library builds without `host-68k`. Modland's `pseudo oops.sun` and `unknown.sun` (a third build whose data block lies before the file) are taken by no player and not here | UADE's Eagleplayer "SUN-Tronic", run with its write and DMA logs (the EP 2.06 "SUNtronic" gives the same writes, 571 to 1956 colour clocks sooner) | All 3 modules over 300 s (and shades2, msx-intro to their song end, 400 s cap): value, order and DMA gaps of every Paula write equal the reference's (replay-cmp); clocks equal (msx-cracktro) or 1 colour clock off (msx-intro, shades2; on shades2 498 of 112,492 writes are 11 or 12 off and 10 are a raster line off); DMA words equal on msx-cracktro and msx-intro (1 of 4.0 M differs) and WAV identical for those two; shades2 390 of 3.8 M DMA words differ (the voice buffers rebuilt while Paula reads them) |

A note start restarts the Paula channel through the original's sequence (DMA off, period 1, two manual data words, sample, DMA on, loop part), each wait polling the interrupt flag on the engine's Paula model at the specification's polling instants; every write is placed at the cycle of the specification's cost model, counted from the tick's DMACON write, with the specification's tick schedule (a late tick runs as soon as the previous one ends). Fitted: the first pattern call of a channel costs 3 cycles more than the specification's figure; the first write sits 680 cc after the engine's start. | Blade Packer (`uds.*` + `smp.*`; Tord 'Blade' Jansson's tracker, the game Obsession; 5 modules) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, no embedded data (`src/replay/bladepacker.rs`). A ProTracker-like sequencer with its own command set (0-F, E sub-commands, the original's quirks kept: inverted E1/E2, signed arpeggio parameter and its sample-header period row, the slot shift after command 8) feeds an output stage that estimates each channel's Paula read position and reprograms a voice only when a note starts (the first-tick fifth-channel quirk included). Subsongs from the song-restart and jump commands, 50 Hz ticks of 70,940 colour clocks, write times from the specification's formulas (raster waits on the absolute clock). Choices where the specification is undefined: a note before any sample, a sample number beyond the table, a finetune above 15 and a command byte that is not a multiple of 4 start nothing or do nothing; a jump or break past the position list ends the list (song end and restart) | Blade Packer (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | Value and order of every register write equal the reference's in all 9 logged real runs (5 modules, subsongs 0 and one later one; about 8,300 ticks) and in the 13 synthetic edited modules' runs (random commands, command E sub-commands, 8 voices, word rows, command 8, pattern break, restart positions); the host's subsong stepping (end-sound and init bursts) is reproduced. | UFO (`mus.*` / `ufo.*`; MicroProse 1994, UFO: Enemy Unknown; a `.mus` song plus its sample bank `NAME.BANK`, else `SMP.set`) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs of synthetic modules; no original code or data (`src/replay/ufo.rs`). The song is a chunked `FORM`/`DDAT`/`BODY` file whose first chunk is a `CHAN` (that is the whole detection; none of the 912 corpus files tried is taken); the sample bank is read as a companion file. A small MIDI-like sequencer: up to 16 tracks of timed events (notes with running status, controllers for tempo and volume scale, program change, pitch bend, call and return of shared blocks) on four voices, a tempo accumulator run twice per 59,115-colour-clock interrupt, a linear volume stage with a release. Register writes are placed on the interrupt grid, the two raster-line waits of a note start as the specification gives them and the code between the writes as fitted cycle costs (the start-up delay from the number of chunks), so values and order are exact and the clock is approximate. No genuine game module was available: chunk walking is tolerant and bounded (an unknown chunk id ends the walk; a stream running off the data, a call of a missing block or a runaway chain of zero-delay events ends the song). Choices where the reference is undefined: notes above 107 (past the 84-entry period table) get the period 0, and the song ends at the first end signal (the end event or the restart program change) | UADE's Eagleplayer "UFO" (the 68k wrapper and the game's sequencer), run with its write log | Value and order of every register write equal the reference's in 209 of the 222 synthetic modules; 12 more differ only in the period of notes above 107 (the reference reads the memory around its table there); the last runs off the end of its data. Where the specification is silent or the reference faults (a CPU error or an over-long routine in a tick ends the song; at load the module is not recognised; a division by zero in the emulator stops the playback; reads of the ST sound-chip port give zero; voice masks other than 0xF unchecked; the services' cycle cost is assumed) the replay goes on safely | UADE's Eagleplayer "YM-2149" ("The YM2149 Player v1.52c"), run with its write log and DMA log; refcmp runs it from a directory holding it alone, since UADE's Soundtracker IV check takes MYST files first | Value, order and absolute colour clock of every Paula write equal the reference's in all 11 synthetic modules (200 ticks each, 36,583 writes) and the DMA words equal; real modules were not available (only `astaroth-game.ymst`, whose replay file is in a pack that is not in the corpus), so the timing of the first tick for real modules (the host's load cost) is not checked |
| Mark Cooksey old player (`mco.*`; the 1988 song and sample data of Aquablast, Live and Let Die, Overlander, A Question of Sport, Mike Read's Computer Pop Quiz, one file equal to the newer player's `mcs.*`; no file with this prefix is in the archive) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code; only the 16 words in front of the period table are embedded, on the project owner's decision (`src/replay/markcookseyold.rs`). Four voices run the byte-coded track language (note, period, instrument, volume, rest, note off, stop, call, return, loops, marks) with the player's own 40-word period table, raw unscaled volumes, and the note start of the old player (DMA off and ten-line wait only on a running channel, then the sample, five-line wait, silence loop). Every write is placed from the specification's cycle ledger, the two line waits from the beam phase. Decisions: subsongs numbered from 0 to (L-8)/16-1; the weak detection (first byte, size field, a zero byte) is tightened by a check of the subsong table and registered last; period indices before the table (code words of the player, read by Overlander's first two subsongs) read the 16 embedded words (`markcookseyold_period_below.bin`) | Mark Cooksey Old (the Eagleplayer UADE ships), run with its write log | Every Paula write (register, value, order and colour clock; LC relative to the module) against the reference's 16 logged runs (13 real subsongs of 4 modules, 3 synthetic, 119,652 writes, up to 3000 ticks or to the song end): identical in all 16 (Overlander subsongs 0 and 1 included, with the embedded words before the period table). refcmp audio: LAG-ONLY on 5 files, SIMILAR on Overlander |
| Janne Salmijarvi Optimizer (`js.*`, magic `JS92`; the replay routine of the music disk "A Taste Of U4IA", Megawatts 1992; no real module is known, only 7 synthetic ones) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/jannesalmijarvi.rs`; the period table is the repository's standard ProTracker one with the specification's single changed entry, finetune row 9 note 23 = 230). A ProTracker 2.x style player with its own cell encoding (note as a table offset, whole command and sample bytes), four voices, whose interrupt runs four times per tick: the tick, then DMA on, then the loop registers, then nothing. Differences from ProTracker follow the specification (empty cells do nothing, the slide mask starts at 0, D takes a plain row number, F00 does not stop, every B reports the song end, E9 never retriggers on tick 0, the sample length is not shortened by its loop). Every register write is placed at the cycle of the specification's cost model; the interrupt period follows tempo commands one interval late. Choices where the specification is silent: a command above 15 does nothing, a sample number above 31 is ignored, table lookups outside the table give 0, an invert loop on a voice without sample changes nothing. Registered before the 15-instrument SoundTracker replayers, whose content rules take such a module | Janne Salmijarvi Optimizer (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | Value, order and absolute colour clock (including the CIA filter writes and the start writes) of every write equal the reference's in 6 of the 7 synthetic modules' logs (the seventh, `JS.short`, tick by tick; 10,210 writes in all: notes, slides, tone porta with glissando, vibrato, tremolo, volume slides, all E commands, tempo commands, invert loop, pattern loops and delay, song end); refcmp IDENTICAL for 6 of them (`JS.short` is taken by the ProWizard conversion in both runs: NOREF) |
| ZoundMonitor (`sng.*`; AJ of Activas, 1988, the replayer of the demo The Musical FruitBasket; no real module in the archive, all validation uses synthetic modules, and the `sng.*` files of the archive are ProTracker or other formats) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/zoundmonitor.rs`; the 37-value period table is the specification's). A song file holds the position table, the single-voice patterns of 32 rows and up to 15 sample descriptions; the sample files are read from the `Samples` directory beside the song (a missing one makes the module fail to load; an IFF file is read with the fixed header layout). Four voices, 50 Hz, a row every `speed` ticks: note starts with instrument load and legato, arpeggio, slide, portamento (which consumes the next row), note off by note 63, track volume / instrument / transpose offsets, and the out-of-range note indices that read the player's state words. A sample started on a row tick is switched on by the next tick, which then rewrites LC/LEN with the repeat part. Every write is placed at the cycle of the specification's cost model (per-step costs, the wait for nine raster-line changes of an effect tick, from the absolute clock). Registered late (the detection is a trailer rule). Decisions: an instrument number past slot 16 loads a zero slot, a note index past the state words reads 0, host volume and balance are not applied | ZoundMonitor (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | Value, order and tick-relative colour-clock time of every Paula write equal the reference's in all 5 logged synthetic runs (23,264 writes, 1,660 ticks), `tools/replay-cmp.py`; absolute times differ by a constant of at most 31 colour clocks in four of them (the host's first entry clock, not derivable). refcmp audio: SIMILAR on 4, NOREF on the speed-1 module (the original is silent in the engine) |
| TFMX ST (`mdst.*` with an `smpl.*` companion; the Atari ST variant of TFMX) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original player code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/tfmxst.rs`). The Amiga TFMX container and sequencer (track steps, pattern rows, macros) drive a three-voice YM2149 register image; once per tick the image is converted to Paula writes (pulse, noise and silent buffers, a digital channel from the companion's samples, a 16-entry 4-bit table), with the specification's channel-restart sequence and cycle ledger. The noise buffer is a generator, not a table. Where the specification is silent (a macro or pattern return without a call, beam-driven riff modes, unfitted row costs) the replay ends the voice or track and goes on safely | UADE's Eagleplayer "TFMX ST" | Synthetic modules only (24 runs): write values and order equal the reference; colour-clock times mostly not (first-tick start time unmodelled); audio SIMILAR on 18, DIFF on 3, FILTER on 2 |
| Special FX ST (`doda.*`; the Atari ST music of Matthew Cannon and Jonathan Dunn, Special FX 1989-92, for example Ocean's The Addams Family; not the Amiga Special FX `jd.*`) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original player code; only two small tables of the player's memory around its period table are embedded, on the project owner's decision (`src/replay/specialfxst.rs`). The module's own 68000 driver is never run: the replay finds the music tables in the Atari ST memory image by byte-pattern search, interprets the three voice sequences (patterns, repeats, transpose, drums, arpeggios, slides, volume envelopes, envelope triggers, kick) into a YM2149 register image and converts it once a tick to Paula writes (pulse, noise and silent buffers on three channels plus a noise mix channel), with the specification's channel-restart sequence and cycle ledger (beam waits and polls of this port's own Paula). Emulation type 0 ("Mad Max") only; the absolute-address variant of the table search was never exercised by a module. A note index outside the 102-entry period table reads the player's own memory in the original; indices -128 to -1 and 122 to 127 read the embedded `specialfxst_period_underflow.bin` and `specialfxst_period_overflow_tail.bin` (indices 102 to 121 give the table addresses) | Special FX ST (Eagleplayer `Special-FX_ST`, UADE's build) | Synthetic modules only (no real DODA file is known; 24 runs, 10 directed and 16 random): register writes (value, order and colour-clock time) equal the original on all 24 |
| MCMD (`mcmd_org.*`, magic `MCMD`; "Testversion by Eagleeye of DEFECT", 1993; no real module known, 22 synthetic ones were checked) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code (`src/replay/mcmd.rs`). Three small player tables are values of the specification coded as constants (the 73-entry period table, the 8-byte default sequence, two voices' start values); no data file. A module is a header, effect sequences, instruments, patterns, positions, subsong entries, sample records and 8-bit samples; four voices with their own pattern lists, a volume sequence and an effect (arpeggio / sample start) sequence per instrument, vibrato, portamento, voice percentage and stop control bytes. Every write is placed at the specification's cycle ledger, including the four raster waits before the DMA restart (modelled from the beam phase). At the wrap of the position list the song end is reported and the voice goes on with the restart position's entry (the reference reads host memory there); unsupported sequence commands end the step, runaway sequences are cut; host volume, balance and voice masks are not modelled | UADE's Eagleplayer "MCMD" (EP206's build differs, so refcmp takes UADE's first), run with its write log | Value, order and absolute colour clock of every Paula and CIA write equal the reference's in all 22 synthetic modules up to the song-end tick (the host-memory quirk makes the last one or two ticks differ in a few values); audio IDENTICAL on 3, NEAR on 1, SIMILAR on 18 of 22 |
| Future Player (`fp.*`; Paul van der Valk 1988-89, the Hybris music; 2 corpus modules, 7 subsong runs) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/futureplayer.rs`; the 97-entry period table is written from the specification's values). The module is a one-hunk executable whose image is pure data (the driver is the Eagleplayer's, never run): header, song table, song, track and command-stream records, sound, waveform, list and modulator records. Four voices, one Paula channel each, a timer tick whose length the song record gives; every S ticks a beat steps each voice's stream (notes, ties, calls, loops, jumps, transposes, portamento, sound change, voice end), every tick each voice retriggers, picks its waveform (single or timed list), computes the period (arpeggio or portamento, two period modulators, detune), the address and length (two address modulators for synth waveforms), the volume and the envelope, and starts its channel after a nine-line raster wait. Every register write is placed at the clock of the original's code (static cycle costs per step, the raster wait on the absolute clock, the host wrapper's start-up rounds written so Paula starts in the same state). Decisions where the specification is silent: a period index above 96 is clamped, a command number above 14 is ignored, a song with no voice track ends at once, a song ends at the end of the tick in which its last voice ends; the first tick follows the host's start-up by the specification's rule plus a rule fitted to the logs for the stub subsongs of a `--subsong` run | Future Player (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log | Value, order and colour clock (relative to the tick) of every register write equal the reference's in all 24 logged subsong runs of the 5 modules (2 corpus tunes and 3 synthetic ones, the long logs cut at 30,000 lines), with the first-tick clock fitted; refcmp IDENTICAL on all five, and the WAV of eight further subsong runs identical |
| Maximum Effect (`max.*` + `smp.*`; the music driver of the game 'Allo 'Allo!, Jonathan Scarcliffe and Alastair Dukes, 1993; no real module in the archive) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, no embedded data (`src/replay/maximumeffect.rs`). The song file `max.NAME` (song entries with a voice mask, track lists with a restart offset, pattern streams of note words, command words and duration words; commands 0-8: nothing, CIA LED bit set and cleared, volume, vibrato from phase 0 and keeping the phase, volume slide, portamento to a note, arpeggio) is read as data and the sample bank `SMP.NAME` (16-byte records, 8-bit samples) is loaded from the same directory (`SMP.set` as the fall-back). Detected by content (the first long, the pointers and the end marker in front of each track list). The original's quirks are kept: songs with fewer than four voices assign their data to the first channels but process the last ones (so they play wrongly or not at all and never report the song end), the four effect parameter bytes are shared between the effects and persist across events, the vibrato phase keeps its masked value, the voice records are not cleared at a sub-song start. Write times follow the specification's cycle model (two raster waits of nine lines per tick on the absolute clock) and its fitted first-call formula, which depends on the length of the module's directory path (the real length is used; the shipped reference logs used 96 characters). Decisions where the specification is undefined: a command above 8 does nothing, a note index above 35 is clamped to 35, a note with a sample number outside the bank's table is ignored, a stream pointer outside the file, a restart offset at the end marker or an event of more than 4096 words without a duration word stops the voice | Maximum Effect (the Eagleplayer UADE (`uade123`) ships), run with its write log, on synthetic modules written from the specification's layout (no real module is known) | Value, order and colour-clock time of every audio-register write equal the reference's in all 17 shipped logs (modules t1, t2 sub-songs 0-4, f1-f7, f21, f31 incl. sub-song 1 of f1 and f2: about 330,000 writes, 0 differing lines), with the reference's directory length of 96 characters; sub-songs above 0 reproduce the host's stop groups and start-up time. The content detector accepts none of 11,339 other-format modules sampled from the archive |
| Paul Tonge (`pat.*` song with its `SMP.<name>` sample bank, else `SMP.set`, beside it; a 1993 game's music) | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification, its register-write logs and call timing; no original code (`src/replay/paultonge.rs`). The song has four position lists (one Paula channel each), patterns of note, length, instrument, volume and command bytes, and 12-byte instrument records (sample, transposition, volume, attack, decay and release envelopes, vibrato); the driver's own two tables, the volume curve (17 words, two of them driver words the original reads past the table) and the period table (128 words, 92 of them past the 36-entry table), are embedded from the original player on the project owner's decision (`paultonge_volumes.bin`, `paultonge_periods.bin`). Each 50 Hz tick steps every voice in the order of the original (period, two volume writes, then the pattern read or the envelope, slides and vibrato); a note start writes AUDxLC and LEN and the next tick turns its DMA and interrupt on, and the audio interrupt handler swaps the loop part in once Paula has latched the attack part (the handler and the race of the original's INTREQ clear come from the emulated Paula). Every write is placed at the cycle count of the specification's ledger (the tick, each pattern byte, the handler), so values, order and colour clocks are the original's. Where the specification is open: the bytes 0x9A..0x9F and the built-in-tune command end the voice, a volume index above 16 gives 0, reads past the known memory give 0; the memory after the song is the sample bank at the offset the reference's host used (so PAT.TUNE8's unterminated list plays as in the reference up to the byte that jumps into the driver, tick 864). Recognition is the word 12 and the three list offsets' rule, so the replayer is registered late | UADE's Eagleplayer "Paul Tonge" (the 68k wrapper around the game's driver, which was not available to this run), compared with the spec folder's write logs and call tables | Nine real songs (8 tunes and the title; one set). Value, order and absolute colour clock of every audio write are equal in PAT.TUNE3, TUNE4, TUNE7 (all writes, with the reference's first tick phase) and TUNE2 (with its own first-tick phase, 98269); PAT.TUNE1, TUNE5, TUNE6 and the title differ in one or two audio-interrupt groups each (8 to 13 writes of 26 000 to 65 000: the original serves two channels whose attack parts end at the same time in one handler call, the port's Paula has the second one a line later, so the writes come in two calls); PAT.TUNE8 equals the reference's first 16 048 writes, to tick 864 where the reference runs into the driver's own code. The first tick's phase differs per song in the reference's host (6 songs at 98306 clocks, 98269, 98346 and 97900 for the others); the port uses 98306. No audio comparison against the 68k reference was possible (its driver file was not at hand) |
| TimeTracker (`TMK.*` with an `SMP.*` companion, or the single-file `*.tmk` of the public collections; the replayer of the Eremation demo "We're Still Alive", 1993; Eagleplayer "TimeTracker") | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original player code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/timetracker.rs`). A ProTracker 2.x style replayer with its own packed format interpreted from the module: fixed 50 Hz ticks, subsongs, delta-coded samples decoded in place, and its deviations (period rewritten at a row start only after an empty entry, 5xy/6xy rewriting the plain period, the global portamento mask that makes the first 1xx/2xx slide by 0, E4x as glissando, the 37-entry tone portamento lookup, the old funk repeat, Bxx reporting the song end, Fxx always a speed), the volume refresh on every tick, the raster-line waits of the DMA restart and the LED left off. Takes TMK files before the built-in ProTracker (which plays the ProWizard conversion of them otherwise). The gaps between the writes of a tick are a table fitted to the reference logs (previous write, write, code path, effect and entry shape), the raster waits are modelled; this timing is only partly the original's | UADE's Eagleplayer "TimeTracker" V1.0, with its write log | Address, value and order of every Paula write equal the reference's in all logs (3 real modules at 20 s, 60 s and whole song, a synthetic effect module, both subsongs of a synthetic two-subsong module; identical in every tick). Absolute colour clocks: every tick starts exactly on time; about 60-70% of the writes inside a tick are on the exact clock (volumes 93-98%, effect-tick periods 63-80%, row-tick notes 80-90%), the DMA restart's wait results are mostly off by the poll granularity. refcmp audio: SIMILAR on the 3 real modules and the synthetic one |
| Protracker4 (`mod3.*`; ProTracker 3.6x/4.0 IFF modules, `FORM` / `MODL` with a `PTDT` chunk that wraps a 31-instrument ProTracker module, `M.K.` or `8CHN`; the only real file known is MOD3.heaVen, DJ Pie 1998; Eagleplayer "Protracker 4.x") | Clean-room, written from a behavioural specification and black-box runs; no original code, nothing embedded (`src/replay/protracker4.rs`). A self-contained ProTracker 2.x style replayer behind the container (the player's own chunk walk over `VERS`, `INFO`, `PTDT`; a file it fails to walk is not recognised here, where the original starts and plays silence; PolyTracker `ptm.*` files are not IFF and stay with their port). Its deviations from the built-in ProTracker: initial tempo from INFO (0 is 125) and the INFO flag deciding whether an Fxx of 0x20 and up is a tempo or a speed, F00 ignored, a length of 0 or 1 written as 0, the repeat block of a row in ascending voice order, a retrigger's last period write, the fine slide mask starting at 0, E0x leaving the filter alone, E8x as a Karplus-Strong pass over the sample in memory every tick, the song end on a wrap or a backward Bxx, Dxx accepting 64, an arpeggio reading the player's own variables past the period table, `8CHN` played as eight voices through the Eagleplayer amplifier. Timing: the interrupt period and the host's ten-line wait after a DMA on are exact; the spacing of the writes inside a tick is the measured 68000 cost of each path, written as costs that add up (previous empty cell, instrument set-up, period lookup index, each effect and E command), not as a table. Paths the specification did not measure (E3x, E4x, E7x, ECx details, unusual waveforms, eight-voice costs) use the nearest measured cost. Choices where the reference is undefined: an instrument above 31 changes nothing, a row of 64 or more moves on to the next position | Protracker4 (Eagleplayer "Protracker 4.x Eagleplayer V1.0 (18-Apr-97)", UADE's and EP206's, the same build), with its write log | Address, value and order of every Paula (or amplifier) write equal the reference's in all 12 logged runs: MOD3.heaVen (4,920 ticks) and 11 synthetic containers around corpus modules (7 modules covering every effect, the INFO flag and tempo variants, an `8CHN` one), 56,214 ticks in all. Absolute colour clocks of the writes (clocks relative to the first write, with the run started at the reference's raster phase): 99.35% exact and 100% within 1 colour clock on MOD3.heaVen, 86-99% exact and 97-99.9% within 1 on the synthetic ones (97.5% on the eight-voice one, untabulated in the specification; the two modules the specification's table was not built from, 99.9% and 99.3%); the remaining differences are one-cycle rounding of path costs and a few rarely seen paths. refcmp audio: SIMILAR on all 12 files (the reference starts later in the raster line than the port, so never sample-identical) |
| IFF 8SVX samples (`FORM` / `8SVX` with `VHDR` and `BODY`; 8-bit PCM and Fibonacci-delta, mono and stereo; in place of the Eagleplayer "IFF 8SVX", which is silent under this hosting) | NostalgicPlayer's IFF-8SVX sample converter (`Iff8SvxLoaderWorkerBase.cs`, `Formats/PcmFormat.cs`, `Formats/FibonacciFormat.cs`) and its Sample player (`SampleWorker.cs`), MIT: `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`; `src/iff8svx.rs`. Not a Paula replayer: the samples go to the recorded-audio player of the AIFF decoder (`src/aiff.rs`: no Paula, no Amiga filters, rate conversion to the output's), at the rate `VHDR` gives; `--player` still forces a 68k player. A one-shot sample ends the song at its end. A one-octave sample with a repeat part goes back to the end of its one-shot part and plays on for ever, and the end is reported each time it gets to the end of the data (so `--wav` stops there); several octaves are played through as one sample. Only the `BODY` chunk's bytes are played (the source reads to the end of the file) | ffmpeg 6 and sox on this machine, sample for sample at the file's own rate, before any rate conversion (`iff8svx::decode`) | All 22 files decode and play (and 25 more samples of the game they came with). PCM: 18 of 18 equal to both. Fibonacci: sox does not read it; the 4 files differ from ffmpeg, which takes each byte's low nibble first and clips, where the format (and this port) take the high nibble first: decoded this way the two files that also exist as PCM are within a mean of 0.3 and 1.3 steps of them, decoded ffmpeg's way 1.9 and 7.0. As the source, the port outputs the first value twice before the deltas, two samples more than `VHDR` says, and lets the 8-bit sum wrap (it does not in these files) |
| RIFF WAVE samples (`RIFF` / `WAVE` with `fmt ` and `data`; PCM of 1 to 32 bits, IEEE float of 32 and 64 bits, Microsoft ADPCM, also in a `WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE` header; any number of channels, of which the first two are played; in place of the Eagleplayer "WAVE", which is silent under this hosting) | NostalgicPlayer's RIFF-WAVE sample converter (`RiffWaveLoaderWorkerBase.cs`, `Formats/PcmFormat.cs`, `Formats/Ieee_FloatFormat.cs`, `Formats/AdpcmFormat.cs`), MIT: `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`; `src/riffwave.rs`, on the same recorded-audio player. A-law, µ-law, IMA ADPCM and MPEG in a WAVE file are not in that source and are left to the players that took them before. As the source, a float beyond -1..1 comes out as ±0.9 and its factor is applied to all that follows, until the next such sample: a file with samples above full scale gets quieter after them (ffmpeg clips instead). Only the `data` chunk's bytes are played, and an ADPCM block gives no more samples than it has bytes for | ffmpeg 6 and sox on this machine, sample for sample at the file's own rate (`riffwave::decode`) | All 60 files decode and play. PCM (8, 16, 24, 32 bits; 1, 2, 6, 8 channels): 45 of 45 equal to both. Microsoft ADPCM: 10 of 10 equal to sox; all differ from ffmpeg, which predicts with a division that rounds towards zero on its own copy of the coefficients, where Microsoft's decoder, sox and this port shift (round down): a decoder written with ffmpeg's rule gives ffmpeg's samples on the 3 files tried, whose mean difference to the port is 15 to 23 of 65536. Float: 2 of 5 equal to ffmpeg; the 3 others hold samples beyond -1..1 and are equal up to the first one, and ffmpeg's samples with the source's rule applied are the port's, bit for bit (sox converts through 32-bit integers and clips: within 3e-8 where nothing is beyond range) |
| ZX Spectrum / Amstrad CPC AY tunes (`ZXAYEMUL` container, `.ay`; modland names them `.emul`; in place of the Eagleplayer "PlayAY" and its `ZXAYEMUL` AYPlayer, which do not work under this hosting) | Original implementation, no source ported: a Z80 interpreter (`src/replay/ay_z80.rs`; the whole instruction set with the undocumented opcodes, flags 3/5, MEMPTR for BIT n,(HL), the refresh register, IM 0/1/2, HALT) written from Zilog's Z80 CPU User Manual and Sean Young's "The Undocumented Z80 Documented"; an AY-3-8912 (`src/replay/ay_chip.rs`; tone, 17-bit noise, the 16 envelope shapes, mixer, 16 levels at the data sheet's 3 dB steps) from General Instrument's data sheet; the container and its player procedure (`src/replay/ay.rs`; relative pointers, songs, points, memory blocks; memory filled with RET/$FF/0, EI at $0038, the stub `DI; CALL init; IM 2 / IM 1; EI; HALT; [CALL interrupt]; JR`, registers from HiReg/LoReg, I = 3) from the public description of the AY file format. Not a Paula replayer: rendered to PCM at the output rate on the recorded-audio player (`crate::aiff::Stream`), ABC stereo (A left, C right, B half on each side), a 5 Hz DC high-pass. Spectrum 128: Z80 at 3.5469 MHz, interrupt at 50 Hz (70,938 T-states), AY at 1.77345 MHz on $FFFD/$BFFD, beeper on bit 4 of $FE; a tune that touches the CPC's PPI ports ($F4xx/$F6xx) in its first second plays as a CPC (AY at 1 MHz, Z80 at 4 MHz, still 50 Hz). Subsongs are the engine's; a song's length and fade end it (linear fade), a song without one plays on. Each 50 Hz frame runs one frame of T-states, whatever the code does. Not modelled: memory contention, the 128K's paging (the format is a flat 64 KB), the CPC's 300 Hz interrupt, the AY's measured (non-3 dB) level curve, the AY's I/O ports, the format's special 68k player field | GME (libgme through ffmpeg 6 on this machine), its own emulation; no bit-exact reference exists here | All 13 files and the 8 subsongs of `slightly magic` play (no panic, none silent). The first note's fundamental equals GME's on 11 of 11 files GME plays (65.2 to 539.1 Hz); GME is silent on the two "AY Megademo Digital" files, which here write 4-bit samples to a volume register every 290 T-states (12.2 kHz). Over 20 s: RMS-envelope correlation 0.85 to 0.97 with GME (8 subsongs: 0.92 to 0.99), long-term spectrum correlation 0.99 to 1.00; no drift against GME over 60 s with the 50 Hz interrupt (0.06 % with the 128K's own 70,908 T-states). Quieter than GME at low volume levels (the 3 dB table). Renders 300 s of `aymd39` in 3.0 s; deterministic |
| Frantisek Fuka's AY music for the ZX Spectrum 128 ("Amadeus": the `ZXAYAMAD` type of the ZXAY container, `.amad`, modland "AY Amadeus"; the music data without its Z80 play routine, which the host supplies; in place of PlayAY's 68000 AYPlayer "ZXAYAMAD", which does not work under this hosting) | Original implementation, no player code read (`src/replay/ay_amad.rs`): the three voices' byte code (notes and lengths, JUMP/CALL/RET, LOOP/NEXT, TR/TR+, NOISE/NOISE+, TYPE, ENV, VIB, LEG+/LEG-, the game hook EXTERNAL_CALL skipped), the volume envelopes and the vibrato tables (FREQ/TONE/XOR) from the format's description by its author (`MUSIC_FORMAT.md` of Fuka's fxmasm on GitHub); the byte value of each directive found by laying that repository's song sources (`songs/*.fxmsrc`) beside the AMAD data of the same songs; the AMAD song header (load address, NOISE+ mask 15 or 31, length, fade offset and length) from the 33 files of Patrik Rak's DeliAYsongs (Aminet); the timing (first interrupt of a note at the pure pitch, vibrato from the second, envelope steps, legato, a rest as period 0 and volume 0, TYPE shifted into the mixer by channel) and the note periods (an equal-tempered formula giving the same 84 periods) from black-box runs of the original Z80 routines in the EMUL rips of the same songs (modland "AY Emul/Frantisek Fuka") on the project's Z80. Played on the AY-3-8912 of the row above (Spectrum 128 clock), the registers written at each 50 Hz interrupt. Ends after the header's length and fade. Not modelled: a voice that plays before its first VIB reads its vibrato through an unset pointer in the original (here zeros: only the period of a tone-off noise voice differs) | The original Z80 routines: AY registers after each interrupt, 10 songs (8 files) against their EMUL rips run on the built-in Z80 | 16,000 interrupts (5 min 20 s) per song: all 14 registers equal at every interrupt on 9 of the 10 songs; ZUB 99.5 % (78 interrupts: the period of the noise-only voice, see "Not modelled"). The NOISE+ mask follows the AMAD header, which asks for 15 (the early songs' play routine, as Fuka's notes describe it) on 9 of the 33 files, where these rips AND with 31: with it, Terra Cresta's register 6 differs on 26 % and Commando's on 61 % of the first 3,000 interrupts, all else equal. All 33 Aminet files and the 7 modland copies play (10 s, none silent). Against GME on the EMUL rips (20 s): RMS-envelope correlation 0.84 to 0.90, spectrum correlation 0.97 (Terra Cresta, the noise mask) to 1.00, first note equal on 3 of 4 (GME's first window on Ghostbusters is another voice) |
| C64 SID tunes (`PSID` / `RSID` files, `.sid`; **only with the cargo feature `sid`, off by default, which makes the build GPL-2.0-only**; in place of the hosted Eagleplayer "PlaySID") | NostalgicPlayer's C# ports of libsidplayfp (Simon White, Antti Lankila, Leandro Nini) and of its SID emulation reSIDfp (Dag Lem, Antti Lankila, Leandro Nini), git e183943, each port under the GNU GPL version 2 (`LICENSE-LibSidPlayFp`, `LICENSE-LibReSidFp`; their files carry only NostalgicPlayer's header and nowhere say "or any later version", hence GPL-2.0-only), with the SidPlay agent's defaults (MIT: `LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`); `src/sid/`. An emulated C64 (6510, two CIAs, VIC-II timing, one to three SID chips), not Paula: stereo PCM at the engine's rate, through reSIDfp's two-pass sinc resampler only, no Amiga filters. PAL unless the tune says NTSC, 6581 unless it says 8580, old CIA, filter on; the per-subsong VBI/CIA speed of the header; second and third SID at the header's addresses; subsongs are the engine's, starting at the file's start song. No song end (the caller's time limit applies; a CPU stopped on a halt instruction ends it). **No Commodore ROM image is included**: the KERNAL is libsidplayfp's own built-in stand-in (interrupt entry and exit, RTS at the jump table), BASIC and character ROM are empty, so an RSID tune flagged as BASIC is refused and a tune that calls KERNAL routines does nothing there. Embedded data: libsidplayfp's 290-byte PSID driver (its own 6502 code) and its 210-byte power-on RAM image used for RSID tunes (values a real C64 leaves in $0000-$03FF, among them the 24-byte CHRGET routine BASIC copies there). Two deliberate differences: a fixed-seed dither (upstream fills it from .NET's `Random` and races on it while building tables in parallel, so its output differs run to run) and portable `exp`/`log`/`sin`/`tanh` built from + - * /, so the output is the same on every run and platform. Not ported: MUS/PRG loaders, STIL, song-length database, settings, voice mutes | the C# ports compiled from the checkout, run with the same settings (no ROMs, power-on delay 0) and the dither held at 0.5 on both sides, sample for sample; the hosted PlaySID Eagleplayer (its own Paula synthesis, so music only) | The 11 PSID files of the survey (9 of full2, Last Ninja and Terramex) play. Against the C#: 25 of 25 runs (start song, song 1, song 2 where it exists) equal for 20 s at 48 kHz; Surrogathora II crashes the C# (`IndexOutOfRangeException` in `WaveformGenerator.WriteControl_Reg`) and plays in the port. reSIDfp alone: 7 register scripts, both models, both methods equal. Against PlaySID (pitch-class similarity of 10 ms frames, 1 = same notes; a different tune scores about 0.4): 0.87 to 0.97 on 9 tunes at their start song, Last Ninja song 3 0.85, Wizball songs 1 and 3 0.96 and 0.94, songs 2 and 4 0.60 and 0.78 (same tempo; the two emulations differ there). sidplayfp / sidplay2 are not installed. Speed: 20 s at 48 kHz in about 1.5 s (13x real time), plus about 1 s for the filter tables of the first model |
| Symphonie and Symphonie Pro (`SymM`, `.symmod`; Patrick Meng's tracker, 1-256 channels; in place of the Eagleplayer "Symphonie 3.1", which stops with a CPU exception under this hosting) | libopenmpt 0.8.7's `Load_symmod.cpp` (Devin Acker and the OpenMPT devs, after Patrick Meng's Java player) and `plugins/SymMODEcho.cpp`, BSD-3-Clause: `LICENSE-openmpt`; `src/symmod/`. The loader is a port: chunks, packed and delta-coded sample data, embedded IFF 8SVX / RIFF WAVE / AIFF / MAESTRO / 16BT / raw samples (through `src/iff8svx.rs`, `src/riffwave.rs`), stereo instrument pairs, the load-time instrument processing (reverse, invert, high-pass, volume fade, filter sweep, low-pass, normalise, over-amplification, sustain repeats), virtual and transwave instruments, and the conversion of the events to OpenMPT's commands with the filter and echo macros. The player gives those commands the meaning of OpenMPT's IT-behaviour player (`Snd_fx.cpp`, `Sndmix.cpp`, `Snd_flt.cpp`): linear frequency slides, tone portamento, vibrato and tremolo that run until stopped, retrigger, sample offset and cue jump, pause and resume, key-off fade, channel volume and its slides, speed, the resonant filter and the echo on the channels whose instrument does not bypass it. Its mixer is its own (not a Paula replayer, not the libxmp mixer, though it is reached through the libxmp port's entry): one voice per channel, hard panned (even channels left), linear interpolation (nearest with `Interp::Nearest`), OpenMPT's volume ramps and pre-amp (512 / channels). Plays the order list once and ends; one subsong | openmpt123 0.7.3 (libopenmpt 0.7.3), 48 kHz, 2-tap (linear) interpolation, 16-bit, no dither | The 4 modules of the archive (`303 emulator`, `acoustic modeling demo`, `altered states 2`, `trancendental`: 16, 8, 16 and 16 channels, virtual instruments, RIFF / IFF / MAESTRO / raw samples, the echo) over their whole length: same length (to the CLI's 0.1 s blocks), RMS within 0.02 dB per side, 1 s level envelopes equal (correlation 1.000, median difference 0.00-0.01 dB), band levels within 0.04 dB, and the waveform itself: median correlation of 1 s blocks 0.999-1.000 (worst second 0.865, in `altered states 2`). The Eagleplayer could not be used as a reference |
| X-Tracker (`DDMF`, `.dmf`; D-Lusion's Delusion Digital Music Format, versions 1-10, X-Tracker 1.x and X-Tracker 32; up to 32 tracks; in place of the DeliPlayer "XTracker") | libopenmpt 0.8.7's `Load_dmf.cpp` (Johannes Schultz and the OpenMPT devs), BSD-3-Clause: `LICENSE-openmpt`; `src/dmf/`, with the slide tables shared with `src/symmod/` in `src/mptmath.rs`. The loader is a port: the chunks, the per-order conversion of the tracks to OpenMPT's commands (tempo in ticks or beats, the effect conversions and their memories, the global track), the DMF Huffman sample compression and the sequence loop. The player gives those commands the meaning OpenMPT gives them in a DMF module, which plays with IT's playback behaviours (`Snd_fx.cpp`, `Sndmix.cpp`): linear slides, tone portamento, IT vibrato, tremolo (DMF's share-of-volume form), panbrello, tremor, arpeggio and retrigger, note delay and cut, sample offsets with the high offset, reverse play, panning and its slides, speed, tempo, row and frame delays. Its mixer is its own: one voice per channel, linear interpolation (nearest with `Interp::Nearest`), OpenMPT's compatible mix levels and volume ramps and a fading copy of a cut note; OpenMPT's end-of-sample click removal is not done. Plays the order list once and ends; one subsong | openmpt123 0.8.7 (libopenmpt 0.8.7 built from its source; openmpt123 0.7.3 gives the same results to 0.01 dB), 48 kHz, 2-tap (linear) interpolation, 16-bit, no dither; the 68k DeliPlayer "XTracker" for tempo and length | 15 modules (4 of the archive, 11 downloaded) over their whole length: same length (to the CLI's 0.1 s blocks; libopenmpt adds a 0.1 s fade-out), RMS within 0.01 dB per side, and the waveform itself: median correlation of 1 s blocks 1.000 in all, worst second 0.965 (`flatline`; 0.985 `trance-formation`, 0.998-1.000 the others). The 68k XTracker DeliPlayer (4 modules) agrees on tempo and length within 1.1 s (its mixer is another: level envelopes correlate 0.91-0.998 on 3 modules, less in the stereo of `acid ecstacy`; it has much less treble, 2-3 dB at 2.5-8 kHz and 6-7 dB above): SIMILAR |
| PowerTracker (`*.pt`; Laxity / Kefrens, the music of the two Kefrens intros by Merge, modland `Powertracker/Merge/`; not the Laxity-Player format of `powt.*`) | Clean-room, written from a behaviour specification (`docs/specs/powertracker/SPEC.md`, `src/replay/powertracker.rs`) drawn up from black-box runs of the DeliPlayer on the two modules and 615 built modules: its Paula writes with their colour clocks, the audio words fetched, the guest memory of its pulse-wave buffers; no code of it was read or used, nothing embedded, and the period, detune and vibrato tables are the values seen played. Four voices, 50 ticks a second; sample instruments and pulse waves shaped in a 32-byte buffer per voice; the write times are a per-step cycle count fitted to the original's (SPEC section 10), its init length included. Recognition: the header's offsets, song length and restart, and the word `01 00` before the lists | DeliPlayer `PowerTracker` (PlayerStore, "PowerTracker v1.20"), no genie | Both modules to their song end (92.1 s, 72.9 s): all 53 759 and 42 614 writes equal in value, order and colour clock, the pulse buffers equal every tick, the 48 kHz WAV bit-identical; 539 of 617 runs (with 615 synthetic probes of 3 s) equal in every write and 540 with identical WAVs; the others are where the original reads past its tables or before the module (arpeggio above note 60, pulse widths above 33, negative tracks), 9 probes with a header word other than `01 00` that only the original plays, and 3 with timing differences of about 12 cc |

Not implemented:

* of the Eagleplayer amplifier, all but the 8 Bit Amplifier's default mixing:
  16-bit samples, its delay, surround and mono effects, its prefs, and the 14
  Bit and AHI amplifier engines (the 68k engines themselves do not run)
* most `ENPP_*` functions besides the DeliTracker ones, the amplifier pokes,
  `LoadExecutable` and `AllocSampleStruct` (the rest log once and return)
* the windows of GUI genies (scopes, analyzers, ModuleInfo): they load and run,
  but intuition and graphics are stubs, so they show nothing
* `ModSave-NotePlayer`, which saves the notes as a module file, fails in its
  process startup
* xpkmaster.library for the players: XPK files are unpacked before they are
  loaded (SQSH, BZP2, BLZW, LHLB, MASH, NUKE, DUKE, RAKE, SHRI and SMPL; files
  with a password or another method are not)
* the DataType player

### libxmp's player and mixer

`src/xmp/` is a port of libxmp 4.7.4's player core and software mixer (MIT,
`LICENSE-libxmp`) for the PC tracker formats only libxmp or OpenMPT play. A
loader fills a format-independent module (`xmp/module.rs`: patterns, tracks,
instruments, samples, and the quirk, flow, event and period modes libxmp's
loaders set); libxmp's epilogue, scan (sequences and their ends), player
(`player.rs`, `effects.rs`, `virt.rs`: envelopes, LFOs, periods, virtual
channels) and its 16-bit stereo mixer (`mixer.rs`: nearest, linear and spline
interpolation, volume ramps, anticlick, full panning, and the resonant filter of
`filter.rs` for the loaders that set `QUIRK_FILTER`) play it, apart from the
emulated Paula. The engine tries its loaders (`xmp::LOADERS`) with
`--open-replayers`, after the open replayers, and renders the mix as it is (the
default volume 0.8 is libxmp's full scale; separation is libxmp's). libxmp's
`pow` and `log` calls go through `aormath.rs`, a port of the ARM
optimized-routines `pow` and `log` that glibc ships (MIT; the FMA build, with
GCC's contractions), which gives glibc's bits natively and in the wasm, where
Rust's `powf` and `ln` are the libm crate's and round some inputs differently;
and its random number generator starts from a fixed seed (libxmp seeds it from
the clock), which XM's random auto-vibrato reads. `vorbis.rs` is libxmp's
stb_vorbis (MIT or public domain, Sean Barrett) for OggMod samples. Not ported
yet: the IT and MED event readers and their extras, the IT MIDI macros (the
filter's other driver) and NNA, mono output. `ptm.rs` (also a libxmp port, but on the amplifier's
sample-and-hold mix) should move here once the ST3 event reader is ported:
`ptm_load.c` on this engine would play PTM bit-identical to libxmp, in stereo
with interpolation, and its output would change then, not before.

## ProWizard

`src/prowizard/` is a Rust port of NostalgicPlayer's ProWizard converter (MIT,
Thomas Neumann), itself after Asle's ProWizard and libxmp: 71 packers and
protectors of ProTracker modules (The Player 2.2A-6.1A, NoisePacker 1-3,
ProRunner 1/2, Promizer, ProPacker, Tracker Packer, Unic Tracker, Pha Packer,
Channel Player, Images Music System, ...). After decrunching, the engine hands
every module to `prowizard::convert`; the first format whose check passes turns
it into a ProTracker module in memory, which the built-in ProTracker then plays
(`--player` skips it). The checks run in NostalgicPlayer's order, also on
plain modules, as Devils Replay and HCD Protector keep the `M.K.` mark.

* Detection, over NostalgicPlayer's test files of all 214 formats and the
  archive lists: 1554 of the 1555 test files of the 70 formats that have any
  are taken by their own format (the other, a Tracker Packer 3 module, is
  XPK-crunched); no file of any other format, plain MODs included, is taken.
* Conversion, against libxmp's (`xmp`, for comparison only): most modules give
  the same patterns and samples. The differences are NostalgicPlayer's choices,
  kept (The Player 4.x's E02, B and D parameters, Images Music System's notes,
  NoisePacker's B parameter, ...); two of its bugs are fixed: Fuchs Tracker
  skips the `INST` mark, NovoTrade Packer's rows end on bit 15.
* Playback, against UADE's PTK-Prowiz Eagleplayer in this engine, 20 s per
  module, up to 15 archive modules per family; state = Paula's registers after
  each tick:

| Family | Played | State identical (before) | specdist median, to 2 kHz (before) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AC1D Packer | 7 | 2 (1) | 1.38 dB, 0.87 (6.61, 1.60) |
| Channel Player | 9 | 4 (1) | 0.96 dB, 0.52 (6.46, 1.41) |
| Digital Illusions | 15 | 5 (3) | 2.35 dB, 1.71 (6.79, 2.42) |
| Images Music System | 12 | 0 (0) | 10.62 dB, 10.54 (12.38, 10.52) |
| NoisePacker 2 | 15 | 13 (13) | 1.16 dB, 0.54 (6.44, 1.36) |
| NoisePacker 3 | 15 | 11 (9) | 1.27 dB, 0.67 (6.46, 1.37) |
| Pha Packer | 15 | 11 (9) | 0.96 dB, 0.30 (6.46, 1.25) |
| ProPacker 2.1 | 7 | 3 (2) | 1.63 dB, 0.74 (6.54, 2.02) |
| ProPacker 3.0 | 5 | 4 (3) | 2.58 dB, 1.74 (2.68, 2.10) |
| ProRunner 1 | 15 | 4 (1) | 1.82 dB, 1.05 (6.65, 2.00) |
| ProRunner 2 | 15 | 9 (6) | 2.09 dB, 1.45 (6.80, 2.21) |
| The Player 4.1A | 7 | 6 (1) | 2.74 dB, 1.92 (7.04, 2.55) |
| The Player 4.x | 15 | 7 (4) | 1.61 dB, 1.13 (6.72, 2.12) |
| The Player 5.x | 4 | 3 (2) | 1.58 dB, 1.23 (6.35, 2.54) |
| The Player 6.0A | 15 | 12 (8) | 2.49 dB, 1.69 (6.89, 2.70) |
| The Player 6.x | 15 | 10 (5) | 2.60 dB, 1.71 (6.79, 2.64) |
| Tracker Packer 3 | 15 | 9 (5) | 2.13 dB, 1.45 (6.88, 2.21) |
| Unic Tracker | 12 | 8 (5) | 2.29 dB, 2.00 (6.93, 2.59) |

  "Before" is the built-in ProTracker before it became a translation of
  ProTracker 2.3D (see Built-in ProTracker): it started with the LED filter
  off and computed fine-tuned periods by formula. Over all 213 modules, 121
  are state-identical now (78 before), specdist median 2.19 dB (6.75). Most of
  what still differs is PTK-Prowiz's: it plays these conversions in a
  NoiseTracker-like mode (the period written in every effects tick), starts
  portamentos a tick later than 2.3D, and does not play 0-length samples.
  Images Music System is the converter's choice against PTK-Prowiz's (Asle's
  ProWizard) conversion: the files' note numbers run from 12 to 47, which
  NostalgicPlayer's table (from 1712, so 12 is 856 and 47 is 113) maps onto
  ProTracker's three octaves exactly; PTK-Prowiz takes note n as ProTracker's
  period n - 1, 11 semitones higher, with notes above 36 off its table. Unic
  Tracker's octave drift was the old replayer's (its notes are now 8 of 12
  identical; the other 4 differ only by PTK-Prowiz's late portamentos). The
  reference refuses Magnetic Fields Packer, NovoTrade Packer, Devils Replay and
  HCD Protector, so their playback is unchecked.

## Using as a library

The open replayers, the libxmp port, the built-in ProTracker, the decrunchers and the AIFF
decoder are a library with no SDL and no 68k player hosting (`default-features = false`, or the
`host-68k` feature off). Rust, C and WebAssembly front ends; full guide in
[`docs/LIBRARY.md`](docs/LIBRARY.md).

```toml
# Rust: a git dependency (the package is named amigaplayer-rs)
amigaplayer = { git = "https://fg.lolo.asia/adminlolo/amigaplayer-rs.git", package = "amigaplayer-rs", default-features = false }
```

```rust
let mut song = amigaplayer::Song::open("tune.mod", &bytes, 48000)?;
let mut pcm = vec![0i16; 2 * 4800];      // interleaved stereo
song.render_i16(&mut pcm);               // repeat until song.songend() or a time limit
```

* **C**: `cargo build --release -p amigaplayer-capi` gives `libamigaplayer.so` / `.a`; the
  header is `crates/amigaplayer-capi/include/amigaplayer.h`, a complete program
  `examples/c/play.c` (`ap_open`, `ap_render_s16`, `ap_subsongs`, `ap_close`, ...).
* **WebAssembly**: `crates/amigaplayer-wasm` (the `dp_*` ABI; protocol and a JavaScript example
  in its README).
* **Rust examples**: `cargo run --release --no-default-features --example play_to_wav -- tune.mod
  out.wav`, `--example list_formats`; `--features archives --example unarchive -- x.lha [DIR]`
  lists (and extracts) an archive and says which entries a replayer recognises.
* **Archives** (feature `archives`, pure Rust, also in the WebAssembly build):
  `amigaplayer_rs::archive::extract(name, &bytes, &Limits::default())` gives the entries of an
  LhA, Amiga LZX, zip, tar, gzip or xz archive in memory, with size caps and no panic on corrupt data;
  `Song::open_with(&entry.name, &entry.data, &archive.companions(i), rate)` plays one with the
  others as its companions. The crates it uses: `delharc` (LhA, MIT OR Apache-2.0), `flate2` on
  `miniz_oxide` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), `lzma-rs` (MIT); the LZX reader is in the crate (`LICENSE-unlzx`).
* `tools/lib-check.sh` builds and tests all of it.

The library is GPL-2.0-or-later like the rest, with the per-file attributions and `LICENSE-*`
texts of the code it is ported from: **linking it makes your program a combined work that must
be distributed under GPL-2.0-or-later compatible terms.** The API is 0.x (documented items at
the crate root are stable within a `0.y` series; the modules behind them are not).

## Licence

amigaplayer-rs is free software under the GNU General Public License, version 2 or
(at your option) any later version: see `LICENSE`. `src/paula.rs` and
`src/paulaout.rs` are derived from UADE's (UAE's) GPL audio emulation, and
`src/replay/tfmx.rs` from libTfmxAudioDecoder (GPL-2.0-or-later) by way of
NostalgicPlayer. The built-in ProTracker is translated from pt2-clone and
ProTracker 2.3F's replay source (BSD-3: `LICENSE-pt2-clone`), and the
MusicMaker replayers from Thomas Winischhofer's MusicMaker sources (BSD), the
AHX one from ahx2play (BSD-3: `LICENSE-ahx2play`), the PreTracker one from
Raspberry Casket and rePlayer's C port (MIT, released with permission from
Pink / Abyss: `LICENSE-PreTracker`), the PolyTracker one and the libxmp player,
mixer and loaders of `src/xmp/` from libxmp (MIT: `LICENSE-libxmp`), the
Soundtracker Pro II one from OpenMPT's loader (BSD-3: `LICENSE-openmpt`). The C64 SID player of `src/sid/` (feature `sid`, off by default) is ported from NostalgicPlayer's ports of libsidplayfp and reSIDfp, under the GNU GPL **version 2** with no "or any later version" (`LICENSE-LibSidPlayFp`, `LICENSE-LibReSidFp`): a binary built with `sid` is GPL-2.0-only as a whole. The other open replayers are ported from NostalgicPlayer (MIT:
`LICENSE-NostalgicPlayer`, which has to travel with them). The 68k players
amigaplayer-rs runs (DeliPlayers, Eagleplayers, genies) and the Rust recompiled from
them (`crates/players-native/gen`) are not part of it and are not covered.
